# BK-53 — GATE 1: take every price off the site, and stop promising a refund

**Status:** 📝 **REVISION 12 + THE SPEC + `§T` + `§U`, 2026-09-09.** ⛔ **NOT
APPROVED — PLAN REVIEW RAN AND RETURNED *NOT APPROVED*, BOTH LANES. ELEVEN
BLOCKERS — read `§U`** *(`grep -n '^## §U-0' BK-53.md`)*. **`§U-4` is the owed
list.**

🔴 **THREE OF THE SIX GATE BLOCKERS ARE IN `§T`'s OWN CORRECTIONS, LANDED THE
SAME MORNING.** The `.js` arm `§T-1a` mandated is **red on arrival** — its first
money shape is `/\$\s?[\d,]/`, whose character class contains the COMMA, so it
matches the two characters `$,` and fires on **six of twenty-five built chunks,
two of them vendor bundles**. The corpus row that fixes the scope **miscounts the
scope** (seven excluded, not eight). And *"scope list below"* **points at nothing** —
`S1`–`S8` never enumerate the ten paths, so the closure is not implementable from

*"outside our service area"* while the fee bills **beyond 30 km** and the site
advertises Leduc, Fort Saskatchewan and *"anywhere in Alberta"*; and the terms
box a customer must TICK claims *"any charge is a link you choose to pay"* when
**Interac and on-site are schema-level payment methods with twenty Interac
payments on record.** ⛔ **A fix was proposed for each and NEITHER IS APPLIED.** ⇄ **THE VALIDATION ROUND IS DONE** *(two fresh agents,
2026-09-09)* **and it changed the gate's DESIGN, not just its wording** — read
**`§T`** — find it with `grep -n '^# 🟦 §T' BK-53.md`; it sits immediately
above revision 12. *(⚠️ **A line number here would be stale by the next edit** —
this document has already shipped four wrong counts for that reason.)*

🔴 **THE THREE THINGS `§T` CHANGED, all already landed in `S2`:** the corpus
needs a **`.js` arm** *(a hydration-rendered price is invisible to HTML-only, and

**scope list** *(without it the gate is **red on arrival** on the client's blog
and `insurance-claims`)*; and a **corpus closure** *(`export const prerender =
false;` removes a page from `dist/client` in ONE LINE, the gate's output goes
**byte-identical to baseline**, `verify-cutover` exits `0`, and a `$150` ships
live on `/contact/`)*.

⚠️ **`THE SPEC` was ALSO found to omit about a third of the gate and ALL of the

## ⭐ THE SPEC — **read ONLY this to implement. Everything below is history.**

<!-- ANCHOR: THE SPEC | grep 'THE SPEC' (the heading carries a ⭐, so `grep '## THE SPEC'` misses it) -->

**Added 2026-09-07.** Twelve revisions, ~7,400 lines, and nine "still current
for…" pointers made this ticket unreadable in one pass. **This section is the
merged current state.** Nothing below it was deleted — the revisions remain as
provenance, and where this section and a revision disagree, **this section is
newer and wins.**

⚠️ **It is a SUMMARY, not a new decision.** If you find something the revisions
require and this section omits, that is a defect in this section — file it.

### S1 · What this ticket does, in one paragraph

The client stopped charging for assessments. **Every price comes off every web
surface**, the refund promise goes, and the weekend surcharge goes. In exchange,
a **new automated check** runs at build time so a price or a bare "free" claim
cannot creep back later. **The visit is free, but a travel fee may apply** — so
every "free" claim must carry that qualifier where the customer reads it, and the
**$150 must never appear on any web surface** (decision 25, absolute).

### S2 · The gate's rule, settled

| 🆕 **Corpus · the `.js` arm** | 🔴 **MANDATORY, and its absence would be a REGRESSION against a hole this repo already closed.** A price rendered by a Svelte island after hydration lands in `dist/client/_astro/BookingForm.*.js` **and in NO HTML file** — measured. `verify-cutover.ts:1029-1030` is `.html \|\| .js` for exactly this reason, in its own words: *"a Svelte island's strings compile into its bundle, so a client-rendered CTA would never appear in the HTML."* **The `.js` arm takes the two bans on raw file text; coupling cannot run there.** Plus a **presence probe** (`verify-cutover.ts:1130`'s pattern): assert ≥1 built chunk carries `FEE_TERMS_HEADING`, so the arm cannot go inert on a chunk rename |

### S3 · 🆕 THE MEASURED CONSTANTS — from a throwaway instrument, 2026-09-07

**`A-R10-4` says these can only be measured against a real implementation. They
now have been.** Corpus: **17 built HTML files + `llms.txt`**, fresh build.

| Class | blocks | units | max unit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `body` | 1,636 | 1,930 | **273** |
| `jsonld` | 361 | 445 | 222 |
| `metaDesc` / `ogDesc` | 17 / 17 | 39 / 39 | 130 |
| `title` | 17 | 17 | 79 |
| `attr` | 4 | 4 | 14 |
| `llms.txt` (line = block) | 33 | 58 | 211 |

- **MIN units in any file: 63** (`book/confirmed/`). Max: 309. **A floor of 40 has real headroom.**
- 🔴 **`MAX_UNIT` IS SET BY CONTENT THIS TICKET DOES NOT OWN.** The 273 is in
  **`blog/what-to-do-after-house-fire/`**. **Max over pages we own is 222.**
  → **Measure `MAX_UNIT` over OWNED pages only, or exclude the blog from the
  corpus.** *This is R10-5 S-2's hazard — "a client blog post can redden BK-53's
  gate" — which S-2 found for `llms.txt` length and which is equally true of
  `MAX_UNIT`. It had not been noticed.*
- ✅ **RESIDUE IS SATISFIABLE.** With a **23-entry** entity table, **zero**
  entities survive decoding across the whole corpus. *The earlier "red on arrival
  on `&rsaquo;`" was an incomplete table, not a broken rule.*

| Shape | Result |

**→ Five approved shapes green, both hazards red. The rule is not red on arrival
and it is not vacuous.** *This is the evidence the ticket has been arguing about

reading.*

# ✅ DECISIONS 17–19 — user, 2026-09-05. These answer both reviews' policy blockers.

## Decision 17 — WHAT IS FREE. *(answers R2 Gap 1, the sharpest lane-D finding)*

**User, verbatim:** *"visit is free except for travel fee if client decides when
confirming the booking, report, actual work etc is quoted at the end of the
visit or after the visit. so the idea is free assessment."*

**Therefore:**

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Free** | the on-site assessment visit itself |
| **May be charged** | a travel fee, **decided by the client at confirmation**, handled on the call and **outside the system** (decision 8) |
| **Quoted separately** | the report, the estimate, the sketch, lab work, and the restoration work — at the end of the visit or after it |

⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE THE PICKER MUST REFLECT.** Decision 15 keeps the assessment-type
picker, and `ASSESSMENT_TIER_DESCRIPTIONS` (`booking-copy.ts:580-587`) describes
a cause-of-loss report, a repair estimate, a measured sketch and lab samples.
**Under decision 17 those deliverables are NOT free.** The picker is now *"what
do you think you need"* — an indication that helps the office prepare — **not a
menu of things included with the free visit.** Copy that leaves the old reading
intact is the fee-terms trap in a new place: the prices come off and the
implicature that the deliverables are included survives.

## 🆕 Decision 17a — W22 FOLDS INTO THIS TICKET. *(found while recording 17; in no review)*

**"Free except a travel fee" means the assessment is NOT unconditionally free.**
The site would say *free* while a real charge can follow. `CONVERSION-2026-08-31.md`
§7 W22 already owns this and has been **untickteted since it was written**:

> **W22 · Disclose that a travel fee may apply beyond 30 km, before payment.**
> `#38` proves it is real (57750 → 65888 = CA$50 + GST); the terms copy's own
> comment concedes the form price is *"false for a job beyond 30 km."*
> **Disclosing that a fee may apply needs no client decision; naming the amount
> does.** **Reviewed.**

**W22 is now owned by BK-53** — it is the same sentence, on the same surface, in
the same deploy. Disclosing *existence* needs no further client input per W22's
own text; **naming the amount still does and is NOT assumed here.**

## Decision 18 — THE TERMS BOX SURVIVES, REWRITTEN, CHECKBOX STILL REQUIRED. *(answers R2-B3)*

**User delegated the call; recorded with reasoning so it can be overturned on
its merits.** The box keeps `FEE_TERMS_HEADING`, the `<ul>`, and the required
acknowledgment; **its contents are replaced, not emptied.** Under decision 17
there are three true, non-price terms, and they are exactly the three things a
customer could otherwise dispute:

1. the assessment visit is free;
2. **a travel fee may apply further out, and we tell you when we confirm** (17a);
3. the report and the restoration work are quoted separately.

**Why this over deleting the box:**
- It closes R2's **Gap 3** — with `NO_CHARGE_LINE` as the only surviving money
  sentence, *"There is no charge for this visit"* reads as *"this is free"* full
  stop. These three lines are what stop that reading.
- **The office can still charge by hand** (decision 19), so a consent record
  retains value; deleting the box leaves a charged customer having agreed to
  nothing.
- **Keeping the checkbox required is the NO-CHANGE option for the funnel.**
  Removing a required field would itself be a funnel change, in the same window
  as the pricing flip — against the one-funnel-change-per-week rule.
- `terms_acked_at` keeps meaning rather than becoming decorative.

⚠️ **This does NOT resolve R2-B2.** Post-flip acks attest to different text
under an identical schema, and only `created_at` and git history separate them.
**The terms snapshot is still required** — see decision 19's note and R2-B2.

## Decision 19 — THE CHARGED PATH GETS ITS TERMS IN THE APPROVAL EMAIL. *(answers R2 Gap 2 / R1-D4)*

**Manual charging is KEPT.** BK-51 deliberately preserved it; the travel fee
makes charging an **expected** path rather than a rare one; and decision 16
says do not remove capability from a client still experimenting.
**Accepting the gap was rejected outright** — with ~20 Interac payments across
two months, a live money path with no written terms is a real exposure.

**The fix:** `APPROVED_*` (`booking-email.ts:738-771`) carries the refund and
deadline terms **itself**. That puts the disclosure at the moment money is first
mentioned, and keeps price copy off the public pages this ticket is stripping.
It also repairs `APPROVED_DEADLINE_NOTE` disclosing the slot-release
consequence for the first time in the email asking for money.

~~**Consequence for R2-B2, and it resolves the polarity question:** the terms a
charged customer receives now live on the approval email … which is resolution
(a), the snapshot, arriving by a different door. A gate over today's constants
is no longer being asked to restore anything.~~

🔴 **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-05 — THIS PARAGRAPH WAS WRONG, AND R2-B2 IS STILL OPEN.**
Verified in code: `resend.ts:94` refuses anything but `status === 'confirmed'`,
then `:106` calls `planForAppointment(...)`, which builds the **confirmed**
message. **`APPROVED_*` is never resent by that route.** R2-B2 is about the
**Resend button**, so putting terms into the approval email does not touch it.

**And the ticket as written would make that defect STRICTLY WORSE.** After
BK-53, the office clicks Resend on a charged `confirmed` row and that customer
receives today's terms (*"the visit is free … a travel fee may apply … quoted
separately"*) beside their real charge, **with no refund policy at all.** The
pre-ticket defect at least shipped a refund policy of the wrong era.

🔴 **THE WORKED EXAMPLE THIS PARAGRAPH USED IS FALSE, AND IT WAS INHERITED BY
FOUR DOCUMENTS.** Every version said *"the office clicks Resend on `#36` —
`confirmed`, `cs_live_`, **$628.43**."* **`ROADMAP.md:387` and `:418`, both
checked 2026-08-26, record #36 as `cancelled`** — and `resend.ts:94` refuses
anything but `confirmed`. **The Resend button is not offered on #36 at all.**
⚠️ **The defect is real; the example is not.** Any genuinely `confirmed` row
charged under prepay reaches the same code. **A spec that argues from a false
example invites a reviewer to refute the example and close the finding.**
See `BK-55.md` §A1.

**Decision 19 remains correct and stays — as a fix for Gap 2, the approval
path.** It simply does not close R2-B2. **R2-B2 still requires an explicit
choice between (a) snapshot the terms at insert and render `resend.ts` from the
snapshot, or (b) suppress the terms block for pre-flip rows and state that their
terms record is git-history-only.** This is the one item the ticket scopes its
adversarial pass to, so it is the one spec that must not be ambiguous.

**Still open and NOT assumed:** the exact wording of all of the above, and the
travel-fee **amount** (W22: existence needs no decision, the number does).

# ✅ DECISIONS 24–26 — user, 2026-09-05. **DECISION 24 SPLITS THIS TICKET.**

## Decision 24 — SPLIT BK-53. And the obvious split axis is WRONG.

**User: *"ok, lets go with the split."***

⚠️ **DO NOT split copy-ticket / gate-ticket.** That is the intuitive cut and it
violates the project's own rule, recorded in `CONVERSION` §7 constraint 5:
**within a claim, the copy edit and its pin are ONE commit** — *"pin first and
the build goes red; copy first and there is an unpinned window."* A gate ticket
separated from its copy is both failure modes at once.

**Split by SURFACE GROUP instead — each half carries its own copy AND its own
pins, so no claim is ever unpinned:**

| | Scope | Tier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **BK-53** *(rescoped)* — **the WEB surfaces** | the terms box, the tier picker, `book.astro`, `ContactSection.astro`, `llms.txt`, the eyebrows, meta + JSON-LD, `[service].astro`'s qualifier — **plus the coupling pin and the `verify-cutover.ts` arms that govern exactly those** | **Reviewed** |
| 🆕 **BK-55** — **the EMAIL surfaces** | `assessmentSummary`/`tierLine` (R4-1), `FEE_TERMS_ITEMS` as it reaches email, the `resend.ts` suppression (R4-3), `CANCEL_LINE`'s surviving render, `APPROVED_*`'s terms — **plus every `verify-booking-email.ts` arm** | **Reviewed + FULL adversarial pass** |

**Why this axis and not the other:**
- **It puts the money and the write path in ONE ticket.** BK-55 owns the resend
  defect, the charged-path terms and every arm that touches a real customer's
  record — so the adversarial pass lands where blast radius actually is, instead
  of being spread thin across both halves.
- **It lets BK-53 drop to plain Reviewed**, which is what its content honestly
  is once the email half leaves.
- **`verify-booking-pricing.ts` goes to BK-53** (it zips `FEE_TERMS_ITEMS`
  against the tier table — a web-copy dependency), **with R3B-B2's correction
  intact: keep the `forEach` and the absolute `tierDefaultCents` pin, delete only
  the two `copy.includes` checks, 42 → 35.**

**Both still ship in ONE push with BK-51.** The split is about **reviewable
blast radius, not deploy separation** — decision 24 does not repeal §4.

## Decision 25 — THE $150 NEVER APPEARS ON THE WEBSITE

**User, verbatim:** *"only spoken via call or the confirmation email from the
owner triggered from the admin panel."*

- **Website copy:** *"a travel fee may apply"* — **and never a number.** This
  settles W22's open half: existence is disclosed, the amount is not.
- **The amount reaches the customer only** by the confirmation call, or by an
  email the owner triggers from the admin panel.

🔴 **AND THAT SECOND CHANNEL DOES NOT EXIST TODAY. Verified:** `admin_notes`
(`db.ts:108`) is **office-only** — it is written by `booking-admin-entry.ts`,
labelled *"Office notes"*, and **renders in no customer-facing message**
(`grep admin_notes src/` outside `db.ts` returns only commit/entry/admin files).
There is **no free-text field on any customer email**, and BK-51 refuses a
non-zero travel fee outright (`review.ts:246`), so the amount cannot travel as
money either.

**→ 🆕 BK-56 owed: let the owner add a line to the customer's confirmation email
from the admin panel.** Small, but it is a **customer-facing write path** and it
is the only mechanism decision 25 names that the software does not have.
**Not a gate on BK-53/BK-55** — the phone channel works today.

## Decision 26 — THE DRAFTED COPY IS APPROVED

**User: *"ok."*** Revision 3 §1 + §5a, **as amended by revision 4**, are approved
for implementation — subject to one change from decision 25: **no number appears
in any web surface**, and to revision 4's corrections (R4-4's re-derived claim
shapes, C1's *"confirmed before the visit"* → *"…when we confirm your
appointment"*).

# ✅ DECISIONS 27–29 — 2026-09-05. **The user delegated all three; recorded with reasoning so each can be overturned on its merits** (the decision-18 pattern).

**User, verbatim, to all three questions: *"whats your recommendation?"*** These
are therefore **recommendations adopted as decisions**, not client answers. Each
states what would change if it is reversed.

## Decision 27 — NO STATED CANCELLATION-NOTICE EXPECTATION. *(answers R5-10's last row)*

**The terms box says how to cancel and nothing about how much notice.**

**Why:**
- **The old line existed because money was at stake.** `FEE_TERMS_REFUND` carried
  a notice window because a late cancellation cost the customer a refund. Under
  decision 17 nothing is forfeited, so **the rule loses its consequence, not just
  its home.** A notice rule with no consequence is either ignored or reads as an
  implied threat the software cannot carry out.
- **It would be a NEW customer-facing rule shipped in the same deploy as the
  pricing flip** — a second funnel change in one window, which is exactly what
  decision 18 refused when it kept the required checkbox.
- **The channel already exists.** The office asks for notice on the confirmation
  call — the same channel decision 25 gives the travel fee.
- **Adding it later is one commit** into an array this ticket is already
  rewriting.

⚠️ **NOT the same as deciding the client does not want one.** → **Ask the client
at the end of September**, alongside the qualitative check-in ROADMAP index item
10 already schedules. **Recorded there so it is not lost a third time** — it was
raised in plan review round 3 and dropped by four consecutive revisions.

**If reversed:** one terms item, its claim shape added to the order pin, and a
number from the client. No structural change.

## Decision 28 — THE OFFICE'S MESSAGE LOSES THE FIGURE TOO. *(answers BK-55's S3; reverses assumption A3)*

**`booking-email.ts:1214` names the tier, with no price — the same as the
customer's. `assessmentSummary` becomes dead and is DELETED with its branch.**

🔴 **A3's stated basis was wrong and the reviewer was right to reject it.** It
cited decision 8 (*"the office decides the travel fee on the confirmation call
and needs the number"*) — but **decision 8 says the $150 stays out of the
codebase entirely**, and the figure at `:1214` is **not a travel fee**:
`booking-email.ts:219-221` records that `assessmentSummary` calls
`assessmentQuote` with **no `travelFeeCents`**. It is the **assessment** price —
the thing decision 17 makes free.

**And the deciding evidence is on the branch, not in the argument.** Verified at
`bk51-gated:[id].astro:1818-1836`: the approval screen's **`assessment_amount`
field is pre-filled `value="0.00"`**, and the hint beneath it shows the **tier
NAME only**. **BK-51 already decided that the office's own working surface does
not quote the retired price.** An internal email still saying *"$699.00 + GST
($733.95 total)"* contradicts the screen the office actually acts on — and it is
the one message that would have survived the flip untouched.

**Why this over keeping it:**
- **The office is not deprived.** The screen they approve on carries the tier and
  an amount field; they type a figure when they mean to charge one.
- **G2 would have been actively harmful.** Pinning *"the office keeps the
  figure"* pins a **retired price list** into place and turns the suite red on
  its removal — a pin defending a defect.
- **It simplifies the fix.** Both callers go to `tierNameOf`; `assessmentSummary`
  and its dead comment at `:450-459` go with them.

⚠️ **This partially vindicates R4-1's instinct and does not vindicate its edit.**
R4-1 was right that the figure should go from the customer's message; it was
wrong that the way to do that is `return name;` inside `assessmentSummary`, which
would have duplicated `tierNameOf` and changed `:1214` **silently**. **The
destination was right, the mechanism was not, and the scope question BK-55 §1
answered with A3 was answered wrongly.** All three statements are now on the
record.

**If reversed** (the office keeps it): `:1214` stays as-is, G2 inverts to *"the
office arm still carries a figure"*, red-first row 3 inverts with it, and
`assessmentSummary` survives — but then **`[id].astro`'s `0.00` default and the
email disagree**, and that disagreement needs its own answer.

## Decision 29 — THE RESEND PATH IS THREE-STATE, AND THE ROWS THAT ACCEPTED NOTHING GET THEIR OWN SENTENCE. *(answers BK-55's S8 and B4)*

**Three branches, three sentences, all three pinned:**

| Row | What it gets |
| --- | --- |
| **Was actually charged** (paid-era terms) | the pointer: *"The terms you accepted are in your original confirmation email."* |
| **Free-era, terms accepted** | today's terms block, rendered normally |
| **Accepted no terms** — `terms_acked_at` NULL, admin-created by design | its own line: *"This booking was taken over the phone. Our current terms are on the website."* |

**Why this over the alternatives:**
- **It is the only option where nothing false reaches anyone.** The pointer
  sentence is false for a customer who accepted nothing, and false again for a
  pre-BK-27 row whose original email had no terms block at all.
- ***"Say nothing"* contradicts `booking-email.ts:501-511`**, which treats the
  block as structurally always present, and ships a message with a hole in it.
- ***"Route to office"* removes a capability**, on the one button that exists to
  recover an email a customer never got — and **decision 16 says do not remove
  capability from a client still experimenting.**
- 🔴 **The branch has to exist anyway.** B4 established that `terms_acked_at` is
  nullable by design (`verify-booking-commit.ts:806`) and that `null < cutoff`
  coerces to `true`, routing every phone booking into the wrong arm. **Once the
  three-state gate is mandatory, giving the third state a sentence is nearly
  free.**

⚠️ ~~Adopt B5's fix and key the first branch on `total_amount_cents > 0`.~~
🔴 **AMENDED 2026-09-05, SAME DAY — that discriminator misclassifies a
population.** Re-derived against the enumerated cases: a **post-flip
hand-charged** booking has `total_amount_cents > 0` and accepted the **current**
terms, so money is a proxy that was exact only while every booking was charged —
i.e. only before the flip this ticket ships. *(CLAUDE.md's rule-rewrite trap,
caught rather than repeated: the predicate changed, so every case under it was
re-derived.)*
**→ The discriminator is `terms_acked_at` against a named `TERMS_ERA_CUTOFF`
constant, with NULL as its own branch tested FIRST** — in JS `null < someDate`
coerces to `0 < someDate` → **true**, which routes every phone booking into the
paid-era arm. B5's objection is still answered: a **named constant set at
implementation and pinned**, never a clock read at send time. **The three states
are unchanged. See `BK-55.md` §C-1 for the full derivation.**

**If reversed:** the third sentence is dropped, but **the third BRANCH stays** —
B4 is a defect regardless of what the branch renders.

# ⛔ PLAN REVIEW — 2026-09-09. **TWO FRESH AGENTS, TWO LANES. NOT APPROVED. ELEVEN BLOCKERS, AND THREE OF THEM ARE IN `§T`'s OWN CORRECTIONS.**

> Two fresh agents, neither seeing the other or the implementer's reasoning:
> a **gate/pins lane** and a **copy/truthfulness/decisions lane**.
> 🔴 **BOTH RETURNED *NOT APPROVED*.**
> ⚠️ **`§T` landed its findings in `S2` this morning. THREE of the gate lane's six
> blockers are defects IN THOSE LANDINGS.** *That is this project's documented
> rate — about half of each round's findings are in the previous round's fix —
> arriving exactly on schedule.*

## §U-0 · THE HEADLINE

| Lane | Verdict | Blockers | Should-fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Gate / pins** | ⛔ **NOT APPROVED** | **6** | 8 |
| **Copy / truthfulness** | ⛔ **NOT APPROVED** | **5** | 2 |

🙋 **TWO BLOCKERS NEED THE USER — they change what a customer is promised about
money.** → `§U-2` `CB1`, `CB2`. **Both were put to the user 2026-09-09 with a
recommended fix; NEITHER IS APPLIED, and the recommended wording is recorded
below as PROPOSED, not approved.**

## §U-1 · GATE LANE — six blockers

### 🔴 GB1 · **THE `.js` ARM `§T-1a` MANDATED IS RED ON ARRIVAL.** Measured twice

`WEB_AMOUNT_SHAPES[0]` is **`/\$\s?[\d,]/`** (`:5624`). 🔴 **The character class
contains the COMMA, so the shape matches the two characters `$,` on their own** —
which is ubiquitous in minified JavaScript.

**Independently reproduced by the orchestrator on a fresh build:**

```
.js chunks scanned:            25
chunks the shape FIRES on:      6
  AppointmentUpload · BookingConfirmation · Navbar
  ScrollTrigger · booking-handoff · gsap
```

⚠️ **`ScrollTrigger` and `gsap` are VENDOR chunks BK-53 can never edit**, and
`S2` states the `.js` arm as a **flat glob** with no scope list and no exclusion,
so `§T-1c`'s remedy cannot clear them.

**Why `§T-1a`'s reasoning does not carry:** `verify-cutover.ts:1029-1030` scans
`.html || .js` **correctly — but its pin on that corpus is a PHRASE**
(`free assessment`, and `TERMS_MARKER` at `:1130`). `S2` generalised the
**corpus** and carried the **money-shape bans** across with it. 🔴 ***A shape
tuned for rendered prose is not portable to a minified bundle.*** `A-T-3` is
right about the corpus and **silent about the pins**.

**Fix:** extract **string literals** from the bundle and run the bans on those.
*(The alternative — dropping `/\$\s?[\d,]/` on the `.js` arm — reopens break #17
for the literal `$150` spelling, so it is not honest.)* Either way the `.js` arm
needs **its own scope list AND its own control**, and it has neither.

### 🔴 GB2 · `S2`'s corpus arithmetic is wrong — **in the row that corrects the corpus**

`S2` says *"the ten OWNED built pages … R9-6 S10 excludes exactly these **eight**
pages."* **17 − 8 = 9, not 10.**

**Independently verified by the orchestrator:** `find dist/client -name '*.html'`
= **17**. `R9-6 S10`'s own enumeration is `about/` · `blog/` index **+ 3 posts** ·
`insurance-claims/` · `404.html` = **SEVEN**. **17 − 7 = 10** ✅.

🔴 ***`S10`'s "Eight" was already one over, and `S2` inherited it.*** **Third
propagated wrong count in this ticket.** ⚠️ **AND THE COPY LANE FOUND THE SAME
THING INDEPENDENTLY** (`§U-2` `CS1`), adding the consequence: **an implementer
building the list to the NUMBER hunts for an eighth page to exclude, and the most
available candidate is `/contact/` — one of the ten owned, and the exact page
`S2`'s CLOSURE row uses to show a `$150` shipping live.** *Excluding it by
arithmetic reopens by construction the hole `S2` calls the worst one found.*

### 🔴 GB3 · **`THE SPEC` still cannot be implemented from `THE SPEC`** — the fix reproduced the defect it fixed

`S2` says *"(scope list below — not all seventeen)"*. 🔴 **THERE IS NO SCOPE LIST
BELOW.** `S1`–`S8` never enumerate the ten paths. They exist at `R8-10c`
(`:3500-3512`) and `S2` never cites it.

**Three further load-bearing definitions are named in `S2` and defined nowhere in `S1`–`S8`:**

| Named in `S2` | Actually defined at |
| --- | --- |
| the ten owned built paths *(needed by the corpus **and** the closure)* | `R8-10c` |
| the **six block classes** — the landmark row says *"one per block class"* and never names them | `R8-2b` |
| `MAX_UNIT` / `MIN_UNITS` **values** — `S3` gives measurements, never a number | `A-R8-4` (`350` / `40`, window `(273,438]`) |

🔴 ***`§T-6` item 1 diagnosed exactly this and is marked "✅ LANDED in `S2` — three
new rows". One of those three new rows is the one with the dangling pointer.***
**Consequence: the corpus closure is NOT IMPLEMENTABLE from `THE SPEC`.**

### 🔴 GB4 · The ban widening is an **intention, not a specification** — and one class has no destination at all

| `§T-2` finding | Fix specified? |
| --- | --- |
| fullwidth `＄150` / `$１５０` | ✅ NFKC |
| JSON-LD `Offer.price` | ❌ **diagnosed only** — no amended key list |
| `attr`'s `value=`, `<option label>`, `twitter:*` | ❌ **diagnosed only** — no replacement expression |
| CSS `content:` | ❌ **NO FIX AT ALL**, and `S2` still drops `style` |
| `<template>` | ❌ **NO FIX AT ALL**, and `S2` still drops `template` |
| the seven isolation-set spellings | ❌ row 56 pins **two** of seven |
| 🔴 **the ten free-claim spellings** | ❌ **NO DESTINATION WHATSOEVER** |

🔴 **The last row is the serious one.** `§T`'s preamble claims *"every finding is
landed at its destination."* **This one is not** — not in `S6`, not in `S2`, not
in `T8`'s enumeration. **And the coupling pin is the ONLY thing enforcing "every
free claim carries its qualifier"**, triggered by `FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES`, which
`§T-2` measured as bound to `visit|assessment`. **On a restoration site the
natural spelling is *"Free estimate"* — the pin does not fire and no ban catches
it. Decision 25's companion promise is unenforced for the site's own vocabulary.**

⚠️ **`T8` is therefore UNSCHEDULABLE for five of its ten items** — a red-first row
cannot be written against an unspecified target.

### 🔴 GB5 · **BOTH `§T-3` relief fixes are insufficient — measured** — and a third evasion exists that neither addresses

Executed against `TRAVEL_QUALIFIER` + `§T-3`'s proposed rejector:

```
rejected   "There is never a travel fee."              ← the ONLY case §T-3 tested
RELIEVES   "We don't charge a travel fee."             ← don't/doesn't/can't absent
RELIEVES   "Travel fees do not apply."                 ← negation AFTER the noun
RELIEVES   "Travel fees are waived."                   ← no negation word at all
RELIEVES   "Travel fees never apply in Edmonton."
RELIEVES   "The travel fee is waived for every customer."
RELIEVES   "Ask us about our travel fee policy."       ← ← A MENTION, NOT A QUALIFIER
RELIEVES   "Our travel fee is explained in the terms." ← ← same
```

🔴 **SIX OF EIGHT still grant tier-B relief.** The last two are the **third**
dishonest satisfaction: neither negation nor hiding. **`A-T-4` states the right
predicate — *"a qualifier that MENTIONS the fee is not a qualifier"* — and fix (a)
implements only the visible half plus ONE spelling of the negation half. The
assumption and the fix beneath it do not agree.**

**And fix (b) omits this codebase's own hiding idiom.** Measured in `src/`:
**`class="…hidden…"` × 42**, `sr-only|visually-hidden` × 6, `<details>` × 1.
🔴 **`<span class="hidden lg:block">A travel fee may apply.</span>` satisfies the
relief and is invisible to every MOBILE visitor — the majority of the paid-ad
traffic `S7` cites.** *The responsive variants are worse than the plain one
because they are idiomatic here.* **`(b) is a blocklist of four class names
against an open set, and the gate cannot resolve CSS** — `.sr-only` was chosen
because it was the spelling the break happened to use.

**Fix:** make (a) an **affirmative** predicate *(the relieving unit must match
`/travel[\s-]fees?\b[^.]{0,40}?\b(?:may|can|could|might|will|applies|apply|added|charged)\b/i`
**and** match no negation anywhere in the unit, order-free)*, with a **positive
control on the approved qualifier** and a negative control per row above. Extend
(b) to `[hidden]`, `[aria-hidden]`, `<details>` unless `open`, any class token
matching `/^(sr-only|visually-hidden|hidden|invisible)$/` **or ending `:hidden`**,
and inline `display:none`. **Pin the selector list's LENGTH per `R10-1`.**

### 🔴 GB6 · The 23-entry entity table is **load-bearing, unenumerated, and `S2`'s own extraction rule kills the pin that depends on it**

`S2` says residue is *"the **only** catcher of `&dollar;150`"*, and row 55 pins
exactly that. 🔴 **`&dollar;` is a STANDARD HTML5 named entity. Row 55 works ONLY
because the 23-entry table omits it.** The table's **membership is load-bearing
and is enumerated nowhere.**

🔴 **Meanwhile `S2`'s `Extraction` row says flatly *"entities decoded"*. An
implementer reaching for a real decoder (`he`, `entities`, `DOMParser`) decodes
`&dollar;` correctly, `residue` returns 0 forever, row 55 is green forever, and
the only catcher of an entity-encoded price is gone SILENTLY.**

⚠️ **Compounding: three descriptions of two mechanisms** — `S2` says *"named-entity
allowlist plus `=== 0`"*, `S3` says a 23-entry **decode table**, `A11-13` says
*"derived at implementation from the shipped build."* **And residue has NO
positive control** — its would-be control is `T3`, still owed.

**Fix:** enumerate the 23 entries in `S3` (or pin `TABLE.length === 23`), state
that **`&dollar;`, `&#36;` and `&#xFF04;` are DELIBERATELY ABSENT and must stay
absent**, land `T3` as residue's control, and change `Extraction` to *"decoded
against the pinned 23-entry table only — never a general-purpose decoder."*

## §U-2 · COPY LANE — five blockers

### 🙋🔴 CB1 · **THE TRAVEL-FEE QUALIFIER NAMES A TRIGGER THE SITE'S OWN PAGES CONTRADICT.** A customer inside the declared service area is told the fee does not apply, and is then charged

**The approved sentence** (`R6-2`'s `ASSESSMENT_TERMS` item 2, `:4222`; also
`[service].astro:79`'s draft `:4306`; also decision 22's verbatim replacement
`:6528`):

> *"If your address is **outside our service area**, a travel fee may apply. We
> tell you the amount when we confirm your appointment."*

🔴 **FALSE. Independently verified by the orchestrator:**
`booking-pricing.ts:123-124` — **`TRAVEL_FEE_CENTS_PER_KM = 115`,
`TRAVEL_FEE_FREE_RADIUS_KM = 30`.** **The fee is charged beyond 30 km.** The
declared service area is far larger and says so **on the same page**:

| Surface | Text |
| --- | --- |
| `ContactSection.astro:203-205` | *"Service Area · Edmonton & **31 communities** across Alberta"* — ~80 lines above where the terms box renders |
| `llms.txt.ts:37` | names **Leduc** and **Fort Saskatchewan**, both beyond 30 km |
| `LocationsSection.astro:26` | *"communities **two hours out**, our certified team deploys **anywhere in Alberta**"* |

🔴 ***A Leduc customer reads "I am in the service area" and "the fee applies
outside it", and correctly concludes it cannot apply. It can.*** ⚠️ **This is
WORSE than the bare free claim this ticket exists to remove: the qualifier is
PRESENT, it matches `TRAVEL_QUALIFIER`, the coupling pin goes GREEN — and the
sentence is false for most of the advertised area.** *Decision 23 constraint 1
warns against exactly this shape.*

✅ **NOT a decision-25 conflict.** W22's text, quoted inside decision 17a, says
*"Disclose that a travel fee may apply **beyond 30 km**."* **Decision 25 forbids
the AMOUNT, not the RADIUS.**

🙋 **PROPOSED, PUT TO THE USER 2026-09-09, ⛔ NOT APPLIED AND NOT CONFIRMED:**
*"If you're beyond about 30 km from us, a travel fee may apply."* **Plus a
control asserting the terms text does not use the site's own service-area phrase
as the fee's trigger.**

### 🙋🔴 CB2 · **`ASSESSMENT_TERMS[4]` claims every charge is a payment link. Three live paths say otherwise — and it is the sentence the REQUIRED CHECKBOX attests to**

> *"We never take money without telling you the amount first — **any charge is a
> link you choose to pay**."*

| decision 17 + `bk51-gated:review.ts:249` | the travel fee is *"handled on the call and OUTSIDE the system"*; `if (travelCents !== 0) return { error: 'travel' }` **refuses it outright**, so the travel fee **structurally cannot** be a link |

`booking-payment.ts:374` — `mode: 'payment'`, no `setup_future_usage`. **That
establishes only that STRIPE cannot auto-charge a saved card. It is silent on

consent record decision 18 kept the checkbox to preserve.**

# 🟦 §T · VALIDATION ROUND — 2026-09-09. **THE SPECCED GATE WAS BUILT AND RUN. IT IS NARROWER THAN THE SCRIPT IT REPLACES, AND ONE LINE TURNS IT OFF.**

> Two fresh agents on this ticket — a **traceability audit** and an **adversarial
> pre-read** with a worktree and an order to RUN the breaks. Neither saw the
> other's output or the implementer's reasoning.
> ⚠️ **This is NOT revision 13.** Every finding is landed **at its destination**
> — `S2`, `S6`, `R8-8`'s preamble, the Status header — because a new revision
> layer is how this ticket lost rows seven times. §T is the *evidence*, not the
> spec.

## §T-0 · 🔴 THE INSTRUMENT IS FAITHFUL — that is why the breaks mean anything

`S2` was implemented as a throwaway *(the adversary's `gate.mjs`)*: the six
R8-2b block classes, text-nodes-only with `script`/`style`/`template` dropped,
comments stripped, entities decoded, whitespace collapsed, the sentence unit,
three-tier adjacency with same-or-next relief, both bans, R10-3's landmarks,
`MAX_UNIT=350` / `MIN_UNITS=40`, residue. **It reproduces `S3` and `S4` exactly:**

| `S3`/`S4` claim | The independent run |
| --- | --- |
| global max unit **273**, owned max **222** | `globalMaxUnit=273 ownedMaxUnit=222` |
| **17** built HTML + `llms.txt` | `files=17` |
| residue: **zero** entities survive | `RESIDUE=0` |
| **6** unqualified free claims | `COUPLING=6` — **and on exactly `S4`'s surfaces** |
| R8-2c's ten in-scope pages, max **222** | scoped run: `files=10 globalMaxUnit=222` |

✅ **`S3` and `S4` are confirmed by a second, independent implementation.** *That
is the first time a number in this ticket has been reproduced by someone who did
not write it.*

⚠️ **Scoring note, and it matters:** the specced gate is **red on arrival**
(today's copy still carries `$399`/`$699`/`$1,199` and four refund promises), so
a break **cannot** be scored on exit code alone — that would be green-washing by
noise. Every break below is scored **twice**: the run's `(exit code, summary
line)`, **and** whether the *planted string itself* produced a finding.

## §T-1 · 🔴 THE THREE CORPUS DEFECTS — ranked, and all three are now in `S2`

### a · A hydration-rendered price is INVISIBLE, and the existing script is WIDER

**Break #17.** A `$150` and a free claim rendered by `BookingForm.svelte` after
mount. `grep -rl PROBEG dist/client` → **`dist/client/_astro/BookingForm.C_k0q165.js` ONLY. In no HTML file.**
Specced gate: **0 findings.** Every visitor to `/book/` sees it.

🔴 ***`verify-cutover.ts:1029-1030` already scans `.html || .js`, and says why in
its own comment.*** **BK-53's gate as specced would ship a REGRESSION against a
hole this repo closed once already.** Confirmed from the other side: the round-1
tree gave `verify-cutover` `EXIT=1`, `✗ 1 check failed` —
*"`free assessment` reaches the browser in 2 built file(s): `dist/client/_astro/BookingForm.DZt-XR4x.js`, `dist/client/index.html`"* —
**the existing script caught the island break the replacement misses.**

🆕 🔴 **CONFIRMED BY A CASE NOBODY PLANTED, 2026-09-09 (BK-57 `§5b`).**
`booking-copy.ts:402`'s *"the on-site assessment and a written scope of the
damage"* reaches **`dist/client/_astro/booking-handoff.JaYwKbSR.js`** as well as
`index.html` and `book/index.html`. **This is a DECISION-20 violation, not a
price** — so the `.js` hole is **not specific to the money bans**, and the
specced HTML-only corpus would miss a false customer-facing claim that is live
today. *It was found by a copy sweep for an unrelated ticket, which is the
strongest possible evidence that #17 was not a contrived break.*

🆕 🔴 **AND A THIRD INSTANCE, THE SAME DAY — this one is a CORPUS-SIZE problem,
not a single claim.** BK-57's two new FAQ sentences compile into

island, so **EVERY FAQ ANSWER ON THE SITE exists in a JavaScript bundle as well
as in HTML** — and the FAQs are **the largest body of prose the site has.**
⚠️ **An HTML-only corpus does not merely miss a planted price; it misses a whole
content type.** *Three independent instances in one day, none of them planted.*

## §T-2 · 🔴 THE BANS — four classes the thirteen spellings do not contain

| # | Break | Result | Why it matters |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **#4 · #5** | `＄150` (U+FF04) · `$１５０` (U+FF11…) | 🔴 **GREEN** | 🔴 **A compound: an entity-encoded fullwidth sign (`&#xFF04;150`) decodes CLEANLY — so `residue` is green AND the ban is green, defeating BOTH of row 55's catchers at once.** Fix is one line: `text.normalize('NFKC')` before matching, which covers the whole class rather than the two spellings tried |
| **#12** | JSON-LD `{'@type':'Offer', name:'… $150', price:'150.00'}` | 🔴 **GREEN — no unit even extracted** | The prose-key allowlist excludes `name`, `price`, `priceCurrency`, `priceRange`, `offers`. 🔴 **Those are the fields Google renders as a price chip. Decision 25 says the `$150` must never appear on any web surface — a SERP price chip is the most public surface there is** |
| **#14** | `<input type="submit" value="PROBED Pay $150 now">` | 🔴 **GREEN — no unit extracted** | The `attr` class is `alt\|aria-label\|title\|placeholder`. **`value` on a submit button IS the visible label.** Same class: `<option label>`, `<optgroup label>`, `<meta name="twitter:*">` where it diverges |
| **#15** | `<style>.probe-e::after{content:" Travel fee $150 today";}</style>` | 🔴 **GREEN** | `style` is dropped whole, and when Astro emits a linked stylesheet the text is not in the corpus at all |
| **#18** | `<template>` cloned into the DOM by script | 🔴 **GREEN** | `template` is dropped whole |
| **#3 · #16 + isolation set** | `The travel fee is 150.00.` · `Travel: 150 CAD plus GST.` · `Travel is USD 150.` · `It is 150 bucks.` · `The fee is 150,00.` · `Travel: ¥150.` · `The fee runs about $ 1 5 0.` | 🔴 **ALL GREEN** | The `+GST` arm requires the digits **adjacent** to "GST" |
| **#13** | `<title>… Book an Assessment $150 …</title>` | ✅ **RED** | **`head:title` IS covered.** → §T-4 |

**And the free-claim shapes are bound to the wrong noun.** Both surviving `free`
shapes require `visit|assessment` in the window, so **the words a restoration
site actually uses all ship green**: *"Free inspection"*, *"Free estimate"*,
*"Free quote"*, *"free consultation"*, *"We do it for free."*, *"on the house"*,
*"gratis"*, *"Zero cost"*, *"It won't cost you a cent."*, *"We eat the cost of
the first visit."*

## §T-3 · 🔴 THE ADJACENCY RELIEF CAN BE SATISFIED BY A LIE, AND BY INVISIBLE TEXT

⚠️ **This is not a challenge to the sentence unit — the unit is DECIDED and these
findings do not touch it.** They are defects in the relief's *predicate*.

| # | Break | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **#7** | *"PROBEA7 The assessment is free. **There is never a travel fee.**"* | 🔴 **GREEN.** `TRAVEL_QUALIFIER = /\btravel[\s-]+fees?\b/i` matches any **mention**, so **a sentence that NEGATES the qualifier grants tier-B relief — and is itself a false promise** |
| **#11** | *"The assessment is free."* + `<span class="sr-only">A travel fee may apply further out.</span>` | 🔴 **GREEN.** The bare claim is relieved by a unit **the sighted customer never sees** — which is exactly the SERP-snippet failure tier A exists to prevent, one DOM node away |

**→ Two changes, both narrow:** (a) reject a candidate qualifier that also
matches `/\b(?:no|never|not|without|isn't|won't|free of)\b[^.<>]{0,20}?travel[\s-]fee/i`;
(b) drop from the relieving set any text inside `[hidden]`, `[aria-hidden="true"]`,
`.sr-only`, `.visually-hidden`.

## §T-4 · `head:title` AND `attr` ARE NOT ONE GAP

`S6` and the handoff both carry *"`head:title` and `attr` have no red-first row"*
as a single owed item. **Measured, the two classes behave oppositely:**
`head:title` **is** covered by the rule (**#13 red**); `attr` is **not**, for the
attributes that carry visible copy (**#14 green**). 🔴 **A single owed row would
close the wrong half.** Both now sit as separate lines in `S6`.

# 🟫 REVISION 12 — 2026-09-06. **THE CURRENT REVISION LAYER** *(⚠️ the SPEC is `## THE SPEC` at the top of the file; this layer is why it says what it says)*. **R11-1 IS WITHDRAWN; THE SENTENCE UNIT AND ALL THREE CONSTANTS ARE RESTORED.**

> 🔴 **Read this before revision 11.** **Revision 11's headline change was
> WITHDRAWN BY THE USER on 2026-09-06 after measurement**, in the same session
> that wrote it. **R11-2, R11-3, R11-4 and R11-5 SURVIVE.** Revision 10 is the
> spec again for the coupling unit and the extractor's constants.

**The decision, in one line:** **keep revision 10's SENTENCE unit and the
three-tier adjacency rule; restore `MAX_UNIT`, `MIN_UNITS` and the residue rule;
restore `A-R10-4`; keep BOTH bans.**

## R12-0 · 🔴 WHY R11-1 WAS WITHDRAWN — three measurements, none of them available when it was written

### 0 · ABSORPTION — **the table R11-0 omitted and R12-5 claimed** *(added 2026-09-07, O3/O4)*

⚠️ 🔴 **T11-9 and T11-21 both named "R12-0's absorption table" and R12-0 had none.**
*R11-0 omitted `R10-0` — which is R8-10 T9 verbatim, one revision later — and R12-5
then dispositioned both findings against a structure nobody had built. This is it.*

| Section | Verdict under revision 12 |
| --- | --- |
| 🆕 **`R10-0`** | ✅ **STANDS.** Revision 10's own absorption pass is untouched by R11-1's withdrawal — it absorbed **revision 9** into 10, and nothing in R11 or R12 disturbs that layer. *It was omitted from R11-0's table entirely* |
| 🆕 **`R10-8a`** *(the count is 24)* | ✅ **Arithmetic STANDS — independently re-verified.** ⚠️ **Flagged by BOTH `R11-4` (the `B*` namespace collision — 8a's buckets are `R8A-`, 8b–8e are `R7B-`) AND `R11-5` #5 (its row-level attribution is an assertion, not a reading: R9-10's six 🔴 are not recoverable from its rows).** *R11-0's row flagged neither* |
| **`R10-1` … `R10-6`, `R10-9`** | ✅ **STAND**, as R11-0 recorded — except `A-R10-4`, `A-R8-4` and `A-R10-5`, which R11 withdrew and **R12-1 restores with their subjects** |
| **`R10-8b` … `R10-8f`** | ⚠️ **DISCHARGED, and 8d/8f are STRUCK where they live** (T11-8). Both replacements failed: the file-level rule on four executed breaks, the block unit on a 20× implementation spread |
| **`R10-7`** *(red-first 45–53)* | ✅ **STANDS** — and is one of FIVE segments of the single table (R12-3a) |

### a · *"Threshold-free"* was false. The knob moved from a NUMBER to a LIST.

R11-1 argued that `MAX_UNIT`/`MIN_UNITS`/residue were unstatable — *"three honest
implementations 87 characters apart"* (A-R10-4) — and that a BLOCK unit needed no
such constant. **It needs a boundary-tag list, and that list is the same knob.**

**Two independent honest implementations of R8-2b's block rule, same build, same
day:**

| Implementation | max block, `dist/client/index.html` | verdict on red-first row 22 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| adversarial agent's boundary list | **9,441** | 🔴 **GREEN — the rule fails** |
| this session's boundary list | **219** | ✅ **RED — the rule works** |

**A 20× spread and OPPOSITE verdicts on the acceptance test.** *A-R10-4 was
right, and R11-7 withdrew it on the grounds that "there are no numbers left to
carry." There were: the list is the number.*

### b · The unit choice is dominated by the ADJACENCY rule, which already exists

Executed over six pieces of **correct** copy — copy where the reader sees the
claim and its qualifier together — with no adjacency rule applied:

| Correct copy | BLOCK unit | SENTENCE unit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `About 30 minutes, no obligation.` + `A travel fee may apply…` (plain) | ✅ green | 🔴 **FALSE RED** |
| **arm `e`** — `A travel <span>fee</span> may apply` | ✅ green | 🔴 **FALSE RED** |
| `<br>` between claim and qualifier | 🔴 **FALSE RED** | 🔴 **FALSE RED** |
| wrapper `<div>` per sentence | 🔴 **FALSE RED** | 🔴 **FALSE RED** |
| `<li>` pair | 🔴 **FALSE RED** | 🔴 **FALSE RED** |
| `<h2>` + `<p>` | 🔴 **FALSE RED** | 🔴 **FALSE RED** |

🔴 **NEITHER UNIT IS USABLE WITHOUT AN ADJACENCY RULE** — and R7-3a's three-tier
rule, as amended by R10-1…R10-4, **is** that rule. **So the unit change was never
the load-bearing choice it was sold as.** *The sentence unit's false reds are
what tier B's same-or-next relief exists to absorb; the block unit's are not
absorbed by anything, because R11-1 deleted nothing and added nothing to handle
them.*

### c · Two of the three deleted constants are the ONLY detector of a real failure

R11-1b claimed deletion *"costs ZERO red-first rows"* and *"loses no catch."*
**Both halves are false, measured:**

| Break | `MAX_UNIT` | `MIN_UNITS` | residue | every LANDMARK |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `<script>` contents leak into the body block *(maxBlock **9,441 → 19,178** — B4's vacuity RESTORED, and larger than B4's 18,154)* | 🔴 **RED — the only catcher** | green | green | **green** |
| block tags stop emitting a boundary | 🔴 RED | 🔴 RED | green | **green** |
| `&dollar;150` / `&dollar;1,199` — an **entity-encoded price**, one of the three figures decision 25 forbids | green | green | 🔴 **RED — the only catcher** | green |
| drop the `body` block | green | 🔴 RED | green | 🔴 RED *(double-covered)* |

🔴 **`MAX_UNIT` is the only assertion that catches the mutation which restores
B4** — the exact defect the whole `dist/` argument is about. 🔴 **The residue
rule is the only assertion that catches a price written as `&dollar;150`**, which
a customer reads as `$150` and which `WEB_AMOUNT_BAN` cannot see.
⚠️ **`MIN_UNITS`'s deletion alone would have been survivable** (the body-drop is
double-covered by the landmark). **It is restored anyway** — R12-0a means its
constant is no more unstatable than the list that would have replaced it.

**→ R11-1b's *"not one of the 53 rows breaks a threshold and expects it red"* was
TRUE as an observation and FALSE as an inference.** Row 33 *does* expect
`residue` red (T11-1), and the `<script>`-leak mutation had **no row at all** —
which is precisely why the constant looked worthless. **A constant with no
red-first row is not a constant that catches nothing; it is a constant nobody
tested.** → R12-3 adds the missing rows.

| --- | --- |

## R12-2 · THE TWO BANS STAY — but call 3's premise needed correcting too

### a · 🔴 `WEB_AMOUNT_BAN` misses **NINE of the THIRTEEN** spellings tested

⛔ ~~14 of 19~~ 🔴 **CORRECTED R12-9 O7: the enumeration below lists NINE missed
and FOUR caught — thirteen. Neither 14 nor 19 is reachable from it.** *The figure
was inherited from an agent's report and propagated to four places without being
checked against the list printed beside it — the copy-inventory trap applied to a
number.* **The finding is unchanged and is still a blocker: most plain-English
spellings ship green.**

Executed against R9-3's shapes in isolation. **GREEN (missed):**
`a hundred and fifty dollars` · `The travel fee is 150.` · `150 CAD` · `150$` ·
`399 plus tax` · `a fee of one-fifty` · `The fee is a hundred and fifty, plus
GST.` · `a travel fee of up to 150` · `about a hundred and a half`.
**Caught:** `$150` · `150 dollars` · `one hundred and fifty dollars` · `CAD 150`.

**Why:** the `\$` shapes need the sign; `dollars?` needs a preceding digit;
`(one|…|nine)\s+hundred` misses *"a hundred"*; `CAD\s*\d` is currency-first only;
and 🔴 **`BARE_NUMBER` is `SOURCE PROSE ONLY` by R7-4/R9-3's own comment, so the
`dist/` arm has NO bare-number shape at all.**

🔴 **So decision 25's `$150`, in the spelling it would most naturally be written
(`The travel fee is 150.`), is NOT banned.** **This is CLAUDE.md's BK-48
negative-assertion trap verbatim** — the GST registration number walked through
`!/\$[\d,]+\.\d{2}/` for the identical reason.

**→ R11-2's verdict stands — the ban is kept and IS decision 25's only
enforcement — but its SHAPES are insufficient and must be widened before the ban
can be claimed to enforce anything.** ⚠️ *R11-2 argued the ban's value from its
job rather than its catch record and never checked whether it can do the job.
That is the same error as scoring it on its catch record, one move later.*

### b · R11-5 correction #3 was itself wrong — `llms.txt` IS in `DIST_TEXT`

R11-1a asserted *"`llms.txt` is in NO `dist` corpus under ANY of the three
options"*, citing `verify-cutover.ts:975-977`'s `.js || .html` filter. 🔴 **That
is the EXISTING `bundles` array, not `DIST_TEXT`** — this ticket's own new,
enumerated corpus. **R9-1 opens: *"`llms.txt` IS in `DIST_TEXT`"*** and is marked
current (T11-2). **The claim is struck**, and with it R11-1a's discounting of the
`llms.txt` launder-by-relocation break, which stands as a real property of any
whole-file rule.
⚠️ **And R11-6's *"the unit change touches the `dist/` arm ONLY"* was false too**
(T11-3, A11-12): a global unit change contradicts **R10-5 S-8**'s adopted
resolution (*"tier A applies to the UNIT (the sentence), and the SURFACE selects
the tier"*), under which a block unit makes tier A's unit on `llms.txt` the
**line** — which S-8 measured as **looser than tier B** on the surface tier A
exists for. **Moot under the withdrawal, recorded because it was a live hole for
the length of one revision.**

## R12-9 · 🔴 THE OWED LIST — **read this INSTEAD of R12-5's ✅ marks**

⚠️ 🔴 **R12-5's disposition table claims eleven in-place corrections. AN
INDEPENDENT AUDIT WENT TO THE DESTINATIONS AND FOUND SIX UNTOUCHED.** The ✅
marks in R12-5 are **claims, not landings**. This section is the checklist.

🔴 **ROOT CAUSE, recorded because it is mechanical and will recur:** the edits
were applied by scripted batches that print a per-edit `OK` **before** writing the
file. **A batch that raises on a later edit discards every earlier edit while its
`OK` lines remain on screen** — and the `OK` was read as evidence. **That is
CLAUDE.md's BK-33 trap exactly: a marker was scored instead of the artifact.**
→ **RULE: after any scripted edit, grep the DESTINATION. Never score an edit on
the editor's own success message.** *(Three edits lost this way — the reading
order, R10-8f call 2 and 8d's opening strike — have since been restored and
verified against the file.)*

⚠️ 🔴 **READ THE LEFT COLUMN FOR THE CURRENT STATE. The last column is
`⛔ WAS:` — the DEFECT AS FOUND, kept so the finding stays legible. It is NOT
the current state.** *A cold-read test misread this table as "ten still open"
because a ✅ row still carried a 🔴 cell — the promissory-note trap reproduced
inside the table built to cure it.*

# 🟪 REVISION 11 — 2026-09-06. ⛔ **NOT THE SPEC — R11-1 IS WITHDRAWN.** ~~THE SPEC.~~ R10-8's three calls are answered by the user.

`MAX_UNIT`, `MIN_UNITS` and the residue rule; move the coupling unit from the
SENTENCE to the extracted BLOCK; keep both bans, rejustified.**

## R11-1 · 🔴 CALL 2 ANSWERED — THE EXTRACTOR STAYS, THE THRESHOLDS GO, THE UNIT BECOMES THE BLOCK

### a · Why R10-8d's file-level assertion was rejected — **executed, not argued**

A fresh agent implemented R10-8d's rule exactly as specified — `FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES`
(R10-1/R10-5 corrections included) and `TRAVEL_QUALIFIER = /\btravel[\s-]+fees?\b/i`
over the whole raw file, no extractor, no thresholds — and ran it against a
**fresh `npm run build`** (2026-09-06; every prior measurement in this ticket was
against the 09-05 build).

| Break, each **asserted landed** before scoring | Result |
| --- | --- |
| A bare free claim planted **99,036 characters** from the nearest qualifier on `/` | 🔴 **`offenders=0`, EXIT=0** |
| The qualifier surviving **only inside an HTML comment** *(73 comments ship into `/book/` verbatim — R6-2's own row at `:3325`)* | 🔴 **EXIT=0** |
| A bare tier-A claim on `llms.txt` with the qualifier elsewhere in the file | 🔴 **EXIT=0** — *and this is **S4 verbatim**: tier A as a FILE LIST, the finding revision 9 confirmed and fixed. **The file-level rule reverts S4 by construction.*** |
| The qualifier only inside a `<script type="application/json">` payload | 🔴 **EXIT=0** |
| `A travel <span class="font-semibold">fee</span> may apply` — **correct copy** | 🔴 **FALSE RED, EXIT=1** |

🔴 **THE FALSE RED IS ARM `e`, WHICH R10-8c CITES AS THE EXTRACTOR'S OWN CATCH.**
R10-8c writes *"the shrunk gate is red-on-arrival on legitimate rendered copy,
independently measured"* — and **R10-8d then adopts a rule with exactly that
property, sixty lines later.** *That is the section's sharpest internal
contradiction, and it is the failure mode that killed the previous proposal.*

⚠️ **Red on arrival, measured on the real build: 6 of 6.** `grep -rlo "travel fee"
dist/client --include="*.html"` returns **nothing** — the string is in no built
page today. It goes green only *after* the copy lands.

🔴 **AND "THRESHOLD-FREE" WAS BOUGHT BY ENLARGING THE UNIT.** R10-8d's entire
case is B4's **18,154-character** vacuity. Its replacement's units:

| Unit | Characters | vs B4's 18,154 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `dist/client/index.html` | **126,013** | **6.9× worse** |
| `.../water-damage-restoration/index.html` | 63,095 | 3.5× worse |
| `.../book/index.html` | 40,374 | 2.2× worse |

**Every replacement unit is larger than the number cited as grounds for deleting
what it replaces.**

⚠️ **One break is weaker than it looks and is recorded as such.** The `llms.txt`
launder-by-relocation break **required the agent to widen the corpus itself**:
`verify-cutover.ts:975-977` filters `.js || .html`, so **`llms.txt` is in NO
`dist` corpus under ANY of the three options** — 17 HTML + 25 JS = **42**, which
is R10-8d's own *"today's build, 42 bundles"*. ✅ **`llms.txt` IS covered
SOURCE-side** — `verify-cutover.ts:733` lists `src/pages/llms.txt.ts` in
`CLAIM_SURFACES`, and tier A governs its lines (S4/S-8). **So R10-8d's charge
that the reverted ban was *"blind on `llms.txt`"* is true of the `dist` arm under
every option and does not discriminate between them.** *Recorded because it was
used as an argument and cannot bear weight.*

### b · What is deleted, and it costs ZERO red-first rows

**`MAX_UNIT`, `MIN_UNITS` and the residue rule are DELETED** — not deferred to
implementation as R10-5 S-1 has them.

🔴 **The justification, and it is checkable: NOT ONE of the 53 red-first rows
breaks a threshold and expects it red.** Row 23 names `MAX_UNIT` only to record
that it **stays GREEN at 222** while the break lands. R9-10 nit 2 notes
`MIN_UNITS` also catches row 23's body drop at 14 units — **but row 23 is scored
on the LANDMARK, which R10-3 keeps.** *A constant with no red-first row is
unfalsifiable; three constants 87 characters apart with no red-first row are
three unfalsifiable constants.* **Deleting them retires no row and loses no
catch.**

✅ **And it CLOSES R10-5 S-2 structurally.** S-2 coupled this ticket's gate to
`llms.txt`'s client-authored blog lines — *"a new blog post can redden BK-53's
gate"* — and R10-5 answered it with a carve-out (compute statistics over
non-collection lines only). **With no length pin there is nothing for a blog line
to breach.** The carve-out is withdrawn as unnecessary; the collection lines are
still **scanned for claims**, which was always the part that mattered.

### c · What is KEPT, and why each one is load-bearing

| Kept | The break it is the only answer to |
| --- | --- |
| **Text-node extraction** (drops `<script>`/`<style>`/`<template>`, strips comments) | breaks 2 and 5 above; **and it is why arm `e` is NOT a false red** — the fragments rejoin as text |
| **Block segmentation** | breaks 1 and 3 |
| **A unit smaller than a page** | all four |
| **R10-3's landmarks, one per block class** | 🔴 **now the ONLY self-check on the extractor.** With the thresholds gone, a landmark going missing is the sole signal that the extractor has stopped seeing a class |

### d · 🔴 THE SEGMENTATION SPEC — **this is a BLOCKER, not a note**

**B4's 18,154-character vacuity is a SEGMENTATION defect** — the astro-island
region is not a block — **and "move the unit to the block" inherits B4 unless the
segmenter is specified.** *Without this subsection revision 11 is B4 with better
branding.*

1. **The boundary set is an EXPLICIT LIST in the verify script**, and **its
   LENGTH is pinned** — R10-1's pattern, applied to boundaries instead of
   controls. *A segmenter whose boundary list can silently shrink is B8's arity
   defect in a new place.*
2. **The astro-island region segments on its own markers**, not only on the outer
   tag that made it one 18,154-character run. **State the markers.**
3. **Every built page in scope yields ≥ 2 segments.** A page that segments into
   ONE is B4 by definition. *This is a structural sanity check, not a tuned
   bound: there is no honest page with one block.*
4. **Acceptance is rows 22 and 54 going RED** (R11-6). **Row 22 has been in this
   table since revision 8 recording B4's GREEN. It is now the acceptance test.**

⚠️ 🔴 **A NAMING COLLISION THIS REVISION MUST NOT SHIP.** *"Block"* already means
two things in this ticket: **A-R9-2**'s extracted unit (*"a tag in HTML, a line in
Markdown"*) and **R8-8a**'s pre-registration unit (*"the pre-registration is PER
NAMED BLOCK"*, whose table lists **pins**, not extracted regions). Moving the
coupling unit to the block makes the collision load-bearing.
**→ R8-8a's phrase is rewritten in place to "PER NAMED PIN".** *"Block" now means
one thing: the extracted unit.*

## R11-2 · ⚠️ CALL 3 ANSWERED — BOTH BANS ARE KEPT, ON DIFFERENT GROUNDS

**R10-8f asked for a justification nobody had made. Here it is, and each ban gets
a DIFFERENT one — which is why the pair had never been argued as a pair.**

🔴 **First, the test itself is retired.** *"Zero backward catches"* is not a
measurement of value, and **applied consistently it retires the extractor too:**
under R10-8c's own stated criterion, **B4 is a defect *in* the `dist` arm** — the
self-generating category the criterion condemns — **and arm `e` is a false red
AVOIDED, not a defect caught. The extractor's catch record under its own
criterion is ZERO, the same as the amount ban's.** R10-8c writes it as *"⚠️
TWO."* *The corrected R10-8 fixed the first draft's bias against `dist/` and left
an unexamined bias inside the criterion.* **Applied consistently, the criterion
retires every currently-green prohibition in `verify-cutover.ts`. It decides
nothing. The BREAKS decide call 2; the JOBS decide call 3.**

| Ban | Its job, stated |
| --- | --- |
| **`WEB_AMOUNT_BAN`** | 🔴 **It is the ONLY enforcement decision 25 has. Measured 2026-09-06:** no script in `scripts/` carries a dollar-amount assertion over any web surface; `verify-cutover.ts`'s only `$` occurrences are comments (`:459`, `:648`); and **`verify-booking-pricing.ts:60-62` positively REQUIRES `$399` / `$699` / `$1,199`.** **Drop it and decision 25 — the user's one absolute instruction — is enforced by memory.** |
| **`MONEY_PROMISE`** | 🔴 **It is NOT redundant with the coupling pin, and S-4 is the proof.** *"The fee is **waived** once you go ahead, though a travel fee may apply."* **satisfies coupling** — claim and qualifier in one sentence — **and is still a money promise.** The coupling pin **structurally cannot** catch a promise that wears the qualifier. This ban is the only thing that does. |

⚠️ **The ~16 self-inflicted findings are real and are not waved away.** They are
the cost of writing hazard sentences down, and **R10-1 has already replaced the
mechanism that generated most of them** (a control per BAN, which survived 9-of-10
shape deletions) with a control per SHAPE. *The rate should fall; if it does not,
that is a finding against R10-1, not against the bans.*

⚠️ **R10-8's calls 2 and 3 contradicted each other** and this revision resolves
it: call 2's replacement was *"plus a whole-file amount ban"* while call 3 put
the amount ban up for dropping, and R10-8 never said whether call 3 meant the
source half, the `dist` half or both. **Both bans are kept in BOTH corpora.**

# 🟩 REVISION 10 — 2026-09-06. ⛔ **NOT THE SPEC — superseded by revisions 11 and 12 and by `## THE SPEC` at the top.** ~~THE SPEC.~~ R9 is amended in place below.

to live copy. **It certifies the LIST and the defect was in a SHAPE; its named

still misses its own category.** R10-1 replaces it.

⚠️ **AND THE PATTERN ACROSS THREE ROUNDS IS WORTH STATING PLAINLY, because it

## R10-1 · 🔴 B-1 / B-2 — THE POSITIVE CONTROL IS PER SHAPE, AND IT IS A FIXTURE, NOT A REPO STRING

**Executed. Delete any ONE shape and the ban's control stays green:**

| Ban | Deletions that leave the control GREEN |
| --- | --- |
| `AMOUNT_SHAPES` | 🔴 **8 of 8** |
| `MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES` | 🔴 **15 of 16** |
| `FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES` | 🔴 **9 of 10** *(independently re-measured)* |

**R9-5a's five controls exercise 5 of 34 shapes. Twenty-nine have none.**
🔴 **And B2 — the finding the whole rule was written for — was ONE SHAPE
rotting inside a ban whose other shapes were fine.** *A rule that certifies the
list cannot see the shape. R9-5a solved a problem one level up from the one it
was built for, and R9-8 row 44 — "change the multiplier shape, expect the
control red" — is the only shape whose deletion that rule catches.*

🔴 **B-2, and it is the sharper half.** R9-5a's stated basis is *"a string that
exists in the repo TODAY"*. Measured:

| Control | Exists today | Survives the ticket |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT[1]`'s *"1.5 times"* | ✅ | 🔴 **array DELETED** |
| the eyebrow *"Credited in full when you go ahead"* | ✅ | 🔴 **REWRITTEN** |
| `FEE_TERMS_ITEMS`'s `$399` | ✅ | 🔴 **stripped** |
| `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0]` | 🔴 **the constant does not exist yet** | ✅ |
| `llms.txt.ts:54`'s free claim | 🔴 **`grep -in free src/pages/llms.txt.ts` → 0 hits** | ✅ |

**Three of five exist; all three are destroyed by this ticket. Two do not exist
yet.** *The criterion was wrong: "exists in the repo today" describes a corpus,
and a control is not about a corpus.*

### → THE RULE, CORRECTED

> **Every SHAPE carries its own POSITIVE CONTROL: a literal fixture sentence,
> written in the verify script beside the shape, that this shape and no sibling
> must match. The controls are asserted as a table — `for (const [shape,
> control] of SHAPES) check(shape.test(control), …)` — so a shape that stops
> matching its own category fails BY NAME, and a shape deleted from the list
> takes its control row with it and changes the list's LENGTH, which is
> separately pinned.**

⚠️ **A control is a HAZARD SENTENCE, not a repo string.** The right test is
*"would a reasonable person write this, and must this shape catch it?"* — which
is exactly the question that produced B2, S-3, S-4 and S-5. **This dissolves
B-2 entirely: a fixture cannot be deleted by the copy change it guards.**
⚠️ **And it repairs nit 5's epistemics only partly, which is stated rather than
papered over:** the control is still a literal chosen by the shape's author.
**What it buys is that a shape which never matched anything, or which stops
matching after a "tidy-up", fails loudly and by name.** *It does not prove the
category is covered — only R10-5's added spellings and the adversarial pass do
that.*
⚠️ **The control table's LENGTH is pinned** (34 today), so deleting a
shape-and-control pair together is red. *That is S9's set-vs-integer lesson,
applied to the thing R9-5a left as a list.*

## R10-2 · 🔴 B-3 — `W53-30` PASSES ITS CONTROL AND MISSES ITS CATEGORY

**Executed. All four ship GREEN under `W53-30` as R9-5 leaves it** — and green
under `AMOUNT_SHAPES`, `BARE_NUMBER`, `FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES` and
`MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES` too:

```
GREEN  "Saturday and Sunday appointments cost 50% more than the weekday price."
GREEN  "Weekend appointments are charged at one and a half times the weekday price."
GREEN  "Saturday and Sunday appointments carry a weekend surcharge."
GREEN  "Weekend visits are double the weekday price."
RED    "…are charged at 1.5 times the weekday price."          <- the one spelling it knows
```

🔴 **The defect is the CONJUNCTION, not the multiplier.** G3 is
`claim-shape × multiplier-shape`; R9-5 widened the multiplier from two spellings
to three **and left the `AND`**, so the ban is still pinned to a spelling — on a
ticket titled *"take every price off the site"*. **BK-55 G3's own row calls
itself *"pinned as a CLAIM, not a spelling"*. It is not, and it never was.**

**→ ON WEB SURFACES THE CONJUNCTION IS DROPPED. Decision 21 killed the weekend
surcharge, so there is NO true weekend-pricing sentence — the claim alone is the
ban:**

```ts
// Decision 21: the weekend surcharge is dead. On a web surface there is no
// true form of this claim, so no multiplier conjunct is needed -- and a
// conjunct is what let "50% more", "one and a half times", "a weekend
// surcharge" and "double the weekday price" all ship green (R9-A B-3).
const W53_30 = /\b(?:weekend|saturday|sunday|after[\s-]hours)\b[^.<>]{0,60}?\b(?:price|rate|charged?|charges|cost|costs|more|surcharge|premium|times|double|extra)\b/i;
```
⚠️ **BK-55's G3 keeps its conjunction** — the EMAIL still legitimately says
*"weekend"* about slot timing (`verify-booking-admin.ts:1161-1167` pins the
`afterHours` **flag**, a fact about the slot, T7). **The two bans differ
deliberately, and now say why.**
✅ **Green on arrival on web verified in the same run** — no surviving web
constant matches.

`head:title` → **the brand token `'YEG Restoration'`, position-independent**, plus
*"the title block is non-empty"*.

⚠️ 🔴 **`jsonld:text` carries the FAQ ANSWERS — six blocks per service page, real

## R10-4 · 🔴 B-6 — ADJACENCY IS UNSOUND EXACTLY WHERE WELDING WAS

**R9-2 fixed welding and kept adjacency. Executed, on `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NAMES`
— which R6-2 leaves UNCHANGED:**

```
[0] Free on-site assessment
[1] Assessment + written report — travel fee may apply
[2] Assessment + report + measured sketch
→ 🔴 GREEN under tiers A, B AND C
```

🔴 **R9-2's justification — *"exactly what a customer reading a `<ul>` sees"* —
is false for this symbol.** `BookingForm.svelte:1155-1183` renders
**name[0], desc[0], name[1], desc[1], name[2], desc[2]** — two symbols
interleaved, one radio each. **`unitsOf` is per symbol, so "the immediately
following unit" for label [0] is label [1] — a DIFFERENT RADIO BUTTON, with
[0]'s own description physically between them.** A customer reading radio 1
never sees radio 2's text.

**→ EVERY SYMBOL IS DECLARED `SEQUENCE` OR `ALTERNATIVES`, and adjacency applies
to `SEQUENCE` ONLY:**

| Kind | Symbols | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`SEQUENCE`** — a list the reader reads in order | `ASSESSMENT_TERMS`, `FEE_TERMS_ITEMS`, `RECEIVED_NEXT_STEPS`, `HAVE_READY_ITEMS` | tier B: qualifier in the unit or **the immediately following unit** |
| 🔴 **`ALTERNATIVES`** — mutually exclusive options, one seen at a time | `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NAMES`, `ASSESSMENT_TIER_DESCRIPTIONS` *(both `Record<AssessmentTier, string>`)* | **NO adjacency. Each member is self-contained: a free claim in a member needs its qualifier in THAT member.** Effectively tier A |
| **scalar** | `FEE_TERMS_HEADING`, `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NOTE`, `FEE_TERMS_ACK_LABEL`, `CANCEL_LINE` | tier applies within the symbol |

⚠️ **The declaration is a TYPE-LEVEL fact, not a judgement call** — a
`Record<AssessmentTier, string>` is alternatives by construction, a
`readonly string[]` rendered by one `.map()` is a sequence. **Assert the
declaration against the shape of the export**, so a symbol converted from array
to record fails rather than silently gaining adjacency.
✅ **This does not disturb R8-3c:** `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0] → [1]` is `SEQUENCE`
adjacency and stays green *(verified: `[1]` splits into two units and the
qualifier is in the first)*. `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NOTE` is scalar and self-qualified.
⚠️ **nit 7:** R9-2's attack literal was written as a three-element array;
`ASSESSMENT_TIER_NAMES` is a `Record`. **The weld and the 84 characters are
exact; the literal as written will not compile** — write the break as a record.

| **S-6** | 🔴 **BK-55 §J's vacuous set is incomplete — FIVE more, same file, same commit.** After §I-1's edit `FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT` renders in **no email at all**, and `verify-booking-email.ts:373`, `:377`, `:396`, `:419`, `:427` still pass over it. **§J names four and there are nine** | 🔴 **CONFIRMED → BK-55 §J-2.** ✅ **And the three `terms`-scoped arms `:290`, `:366`, `:384` are NOT vacuous — measured, each still passes over the post-§B-2 render.** *An enumeration that is measured in both directions* |

R6-2's line 54 is safe either way *(verified)* **but the rule was not, and a rule that is only safe against today's copy is B9's finding wearing a different hat** |

### R10-5a · The shapes, third pass

```ts
// FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES
  /\bno[\s-]+(?:commitments?|strings|costs?|charges?|fees?)\b/i,          // S-3: plural + hyphen, copied from [3]
  /\b(?:pay nothing|costs? (?:you )?nothing)\b/i,                          // S-4: waived/complimentary MOVED OUT
// MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES  (+8, S-4 and S-5)
  /\b(?:waived?|waiver|complimentary)\b/i,                                 // S-4: a conditional waiver is a money promise
  /\bdeducts?(?:ed|ing)?\b[^.<>]{0,30}?\b(?:invoice|bill|quote|total|job)\b/i,
  /\bknocks?\s+it\s+off\b/i,
  /\bsubtracts?\b[^.<>]{0,30}?\b(?:invoice|bill|quote|total|job)\b/i,
  /\brolled\s+into\b[^.<>]{0,30}?\b(?:job|price|quote|bill)\b/i,
  /\bout\s+of\s+pocket\b/i,
  /\bcomes?\s+out\s+of\b[^.<>]{0,30}?\b(?:job|price|quote|bill)\b/i,
  /\bcredits?\s+it\b/i,                                                    // bare present tense
```
**Each of the eleven gets its own control per R10-1.** ⚠️ **The control table's
length is the number pinned at R10-1 — 34 at the time that pin was written, and
it MUST be re-counted in the same edit that adds these eleven.** *Two numbers for
one table (34 and 45) are live in this ticket; **the pin is whatever the shipped
list actually contains**, asserted, not transcribed.*

## R10-8 · 🔴 FOR THE PLAN REVIEWER — **and its first draft was WRONG BY THIRTEEN**

⚠️ 🔴 **THIS SECTION WAS AUDITED BY A FRESH AGENT AND SUBSTANTIALLY REFUTED. The
refutation is adopted.** Its first draft claimed *"eleven of eleven blockers in
rounds 2 and 3 were in the GATE, none in the copy"*, named the amount ban and
the money-promise ban as *"cheap … and have caught real defects"*, and built a
scope recommendation on a claim about R6-3c that is **false and would ship a red
gate on day one.** All three are corrected below. *A refutation is a
measurement — and so is the argument a refutation is used to support.*

⚠️ 🔴 **NAMESPACE DECLARATION, added revision 11 (R11-4). `R7-B` HAS `B1–B10`
AND `R8-A` HAS A SEPARATE `B1–B5`.** **8a's buckets use `R8A-`; 8b, 8c and 8d
use `R7B-`.** So 8a's *"`dist/` extractor: B1, B4"* and 8c's *"the extractor's
two catches are B4 and arm `e`"* **are not talking about the same `B4`** —
`R7B-B4` is the 18,154 vacuity. **Prefixes are `R7B-` and `R8A-` throughout;
an unprefixed `B*` below is `R7B-` unless it sits in 8a's buckets.**
*R9-10's "No ID collisions" checked the `A*` namespace only.*

### 8a · The count is **24**, not 11 — and the ticket's own headers say so

⚠️ 🔴 **[R11-5 #5: *"the ticket's own headers say so"* is true of THREE of the four
counts. `R8-A`'s header states none — its 5 comes from prose. And R9-10's six 🔴 are
not recoverable from its rows: only **U3, U5, U14** carry a 🔴 marker, while the bucket
below names U5, U8, U9, U11, U13, U14 — excluding the explicitly-marked U3. The total
is right; the ROW-LEVEL attribution is an assertion, not a reading — in the section
whose thesis is that a count must be read rather than asserted.]**

**Eleven counts only the two ADVERSARIAL reports** (R8-A's B1–B5, R9-A's

**Shrinking removes the only production-artifact assertion from a ticket whose

   `RECEIVED_EMAILED_LINE` is in **0** built HTML files (R8-2g);
   ⛔ ~~`BookingConfirmation.svelte:174`/`:220`'s hardcoded prose is in **none** of

This point rests on ONE live exhibit, not two** — *CLAUDE.md's BK-36 island fallback-card surface by
   name*; and R7B-B2's general form is *"a scan of those five files sees identifier
