## R9-1 · 🔴 B1 — THE EXTRACTOR'S SELF-CHECKS ARE RED ON ARRIVAL ON `llms.txt`, AND R8-2a's OWN TABLE SAYS SO

**Executed, twice, independently.** `llms.txt` is in `DIST_TEXT` — R7-4 calls it
*"the sharpest of the three"* — and it is **plain text**, so R8-2b's block
extraction, which is defined entirely in terms of HTML tags, cannot shorten a
single unit of it:

| Check | Constant | `llms.txt`, measured | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `units >= MIN_UNITS` | 40 | **38** | 🔴 **RED** |
| `maxLen <= MAX_UNIT` | 350 | **397** | 🔴 **RED** |
| `LANDMARK` present | a **footer** sentence | **absent — `llms.txt` has no footer** | 🔴 **RED** |
| `residue` | — | clean | ✅ |

🔴 **R8-2a's own table records `llms.txt` as 38 units, max 397 — two subsections
above the constants that forbid both numbers.** *The measurement and the
threshold it contradicts were written into the same section, and neither the
section nor a validator compared them. R8-2b's "eight-file corpus … max 222,
min units/file 57" is arithmetically impossible with `llms.txt` inside it.*

⚠️ **And every obvious repair is forbidden or destructive:** raising `MAX_UNIT`
past 397 lands outside R8-2c's measured window `(273, 438]` and is the *"nudge"*
A-R8-4 prohibits; lowering `MIN_UNITS` below 38 guts the check; exempting
`llms.txt` from the landmark exempts the surface the corpus exists for.

### → THE FIX: `llms.txt` IS LINE-ORIENTED, AND A LINE IS A BLOCK

**R8-2b's block rule was written for HTML and silently applied to a `.txt` file.
The rule generalises correctly the moment it is stated in terms of the format:**

> **A BLOCK BOUNDARY is whatever the format uses to separate one readable unit
> from the next: a block-level TAG in HTML, and a LINE BREAK in `llms.txt`
> (Markdown, where a line break IS the block).** ⚠️ **This does not reopen
> R8-2b's newline rule** — that forbids treating a *source line-wrap inside an
> HTML text node* as a boundary. **In Markdown the line is not a wrap; it is the
> structure.**

**Measured with the line as a block:**

| | units | max | p50 | p90 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `llms.txt`, naive / R8-2b as written | 38 | **397** | 106 | 193 |
| `llms.txt`, **line-as-block** | ✅ **58** | ✅ **211** | 59 | 173 |

🔴 **CORRECTED 2026-09-06 (R9-10 U5) — THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS PARAGRAPH CITED A
TABLE THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** It said 58/211 *"reconciles exactly with R8-2's
original measurement table"*. **R8-2 contains no such table** — its only
`llms.txt` row is R8-2a's naive **38 / 397**, which this section quotes three
lines above, and `grep -n "211"` over this file returns only R9-1's own lines.
**58/211 comes from the extractor measurement R8-2 was WRITTEN FROM AND NEVER
TRANSCRIBED.**
**The consequence, stated rather than softened:** the line-as-block rule is
**NEW**, not merely undocumented. *The first draft framed it as a step that was
performed and left unwritten, which is a smaller claim and a false one.*
⚠️ **And the missing transcription is itself the defect that produced B1.** R8-2
transcribed the NAIVE per-file table and not the EXTRACTED one — **and the
extracted table's `llms.txt` row is exactly the number that contradicts R8-2c's
constants.** *R7-A 🔴2's transcription rule was written about findings; it
applies to MEASUREMENTS, and this is what it costs when it does not.*

### → AND THE SELF-CHECKS ARE PER CORPUS CLASS, NOT GLOBAL

**A threshold measured on one population and applied to another is the error
above, generalised.** Three classes, each with its own constants and its own
landmark, each measured:

| Class | Members | `MIN_UNITS` | `MAX_UNIT` | LANDMARK |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **built HTML** | the ten pages of R8-10c | **40** *(floor 57 measured)* | **350** *(window `(273,438]`)* | the footer sentence — present on all 17 |
| **`llms.txt`** | itself | **45** *(58 measured; a 22% floor, same ratio as HTML's)* | **280** *(211 measured; the same 33% headroom)* | 🆕 **`'This is the way to request a quote.'`** — `llms.txt.ts:54`'s own closing sentence, which R6-2 keeps |
| **`termsBundles`** | content-selected `.js` | — | — | 🆕 **`TERMS_MARKER` itself** — the chunk is selected on it, so its absence means the corpus is empty *(the existing `:1136-1139` guard)* |

⚠️ **Each landmark must be a string the ticket does NOT change**, or the pin
reddens on its own copy edit. ✅ Verified: the footer sentence and
*"This is the way to request a quote."* are both outside every edit in R6-2 and
R8-3c.

## R9-2 · 🔴 B5 — `join('\n')` WELDS MEMBERS, AND R8-3b CLAIMED IT CANNOT

**R8-3b, verbatim:** *"the newline keeps the split honest at member boundaries
and **cannot manufacture a claim across one**."* 🔴 **R7-B measured that false
before revision 8 was written** — its ⚠️ under B4: *"`FEE_TERMS_HEADING` has no
terminal punctuation, so `join('\n')` still yields `'Assessment terms\nThe
on-site assessment visit is free.'` as one unit."* **Revision 8 restated the
claim instead of dispositioning the measurement.**

**Executed, on the real constants:**

| Constant | members | units under `join('\n')` + `/(?<=[.!?])\s+/` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NAMES` | 3 | 🔴 **1** — 84 characters, one "sentence" |
| `HAVE_READY_ITEMS` | 5 | 🔴 **1** — 165 characters |
| `FEE_TERMS_ITEMS` | 3 | 🔴 **2 — of 333 and 59 characters** ⇄ **CORRECTED (R9-10 U8): the first draft said *"7 (its members end in `.`)"*, which is true of R6-2's POST-REWRITE draft and false of the live constants — none of the three ends in `.` today.** *The row presented as the harmless control is, right now, the LARGEST weld of the three, which strengthens this finding rather than weakening it* |

🔴 **THE ATTACK, EXECUTED — it defeats tier C, the strictest rule there is.**
Set `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NAMES` to
`['Free on-site assessment', 'Assessment + written report', 'Assessment + report + measured sketch — travel fee may apply']`
and the three weld into **one unit** carrying free shape `[0]` **and** the
qualifier. **Green under tiers A, B and C** — a bare unqualified free claim on
the radio labels, "qualified" from two members away.

### → THE UNIT IS BUILT PER MEMBER. THERE IS NO JOIN.

```
unitsOf(symbol) = symbol.members.flatMap(m => m.split(/(?<=[.!?])\s+/))
```
**A member boundary is ALWAYS a unit boundary, whether or not the member ends in
punctuation.** Adjacency for tier B's *"immediately following"* is then computed
over that ordered list — **which is exactly what a customer reading a `<ul>`
sees, and it is what R8-3b meant and did not say.**
⚠️ **`join` is forbidden by name in the pin's comment**, with this attack as the
reason. *Two revisions asserted a join was safe; one measured it twice.*
✅ **This does not disturb R8-3c's verdicts** — re-run under per-member units:
`ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0]`→`[1]` green (tier B, adjacent members), `[3]`'s amendment
green (self-qualified), the meta description green (tier A, one sentence).

## R9-3 · 🔴 B3 — THE AMOUNT BAN CANNOT SEE A SINGLE CONSTANT VALUE, AND TWO ORDERED SHAPES WERE DROPPED

🔴 **R8-1 lists `WEB_PROSE`'s consumers as *"coupling, money-promise,
weekend"* — the AMOUNT ban is not among them**, and R8-5b scopes it to
`TEMPLATE_PROSE` alone. **Constant values are in neither.** Executed, planted
into `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[1]`:

| Planted | `TEMPLATE_PROSE` | `DIST_AMOUNT` | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| *"a **150 dollar** travel fee may apply"* | blind | MISS — no `$` | 🔴 **GREEN** |
| *"a travel fee of **150 dollars**"* | blind | MISS | 🔴 **GREEN** |
| *"the assessment is **399 plus GST**"* | blind | MISS | 🔴 **GREEN** |
| *"a travel fee of up to **150**"* | blind | MISS | 🔴 **GREEN** |

🔴 **The first is R8-8 ROW 17's OWN STRING.** R8-5b planted it *"in a text
node"*, where it goes red — **so row 17 would have been logged red-observed
while the actual hazard shipped.** *A red-first row that plants the break
somewhere other than where the ticket puts the copy measures the wrong thing.*

🔴 **AND R7-4 ORDERED THREE SHAPE ADDITIONS; R8-5 CARRIED ONE.**
*"add `/\b\d[\d,]*\s*dollars?\b/i`, `/\bCAD\s*\d/i`, and — inside
`WEB_CLAIM_SURFACES` only — a bare `/\b\d{3,}\b/`."* **R8-5 discusses only the
third, and R8-0's absorption row for R7-4 says *"⇄ Amended — R8-5 (B9, B10,
SF3, SF4)"* — SF4 is the finding that produced the first two.** *Fourth
consecutive revision to drop a row from the round it answers, inside the pass
that exists to stop it.*

**→ FIXED, both halves:**

1. **`WEB_AMOUNT_BAN` runs over `WEB_PROSE`, all three halves** — the same
   corpus as the other three bans. **`WEB_PROSE` has FOUR consumers, and the
   list is written once, in one place, so it cannot drift again.**
2. **The shape set is one list used on every half**, with the bare-number arm
   restricted to source prose as R7-4 ordered:
```ts
const AMOUNT_SHAPES = [
  /\$\s?\d{2,}/, /\$\s?\d{1,3},\d{3}/, /\$\s?\d+\.\d{2}/, /\$\s?0\b/,   // DIST_AMOUNT (R8-5c)
  /\b\d[\d,]*\s*dollars?\b/i,                    // R7-4, DROPPED BY R8-5 — restored
  /\bCAD\s*\d/i,                                 // R7-4, DROPPED BY R8-5 — restored
  /\b\d[\d,]*\s*(?:\+\s*GST|plus GST)\b/i,
  /\b(?:one|two|three|four|five|six|seven|eight|nine)\s+hundred\b/i,
];
const BARE_NUMBER = /\b\d{3,}\b/;   // SOURCE PROSE ONLY -- R7-4, and B9's 34 is why
```
⚠️ 🔴 **`\d{4}` remains FORBIDDEN as a carve-out** (R8-5a) — it is a hole the
size of `$1,199`. **The only carve-out is the support phone, in BOTH spellings
(S1).**

🔴 **ARRIVAL COLOUR, MEASURED — R9-10 U14, and R8-4b's own standing rule required
this and the first draft omitted it.** Widening the corpus from one half to three
makes **two of the three RED ON ARRIVAL**, deliberately:

| Half | Today | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `TERMS_TEXT` | 🔴 **RED — 3 hits** | `FEE_TERMS_ITEMS` carries `$399 + GST`, `$699 + GST`, `$1,199 + GST` (`booking-copy.ts:401-405`) |
| `DIST_TEXT` | 🔴 **RED** — `book/index.html` and `index.html`, 2 shapes each | the same figures, built |
| `TEMPLATE_PROSE` | ✅ green *(one hit: `tel:+17804793285` — S1's carve-out)* | |

**This is R6-3d's *"red TODAY, deliberately"* extended to the widened corpus, and
it fixes the ORDER: the ban is observed RED, then the copy lands, then it is
observed GREEN.** ⚠️ **A ban introduced already-green over a corpus nobody
measured is a ban with no evidence it can fire — which is B9's finding, in the
direction nobody checks.**

## R9-4 · 🔴 B4 — A POSITIVE TWIN FOR ONE BLOCK CLASS IN FIVE

R8-2c tested seven mutations of the *splitter* and **zero of the block
enumeration**, then concluded four checks suffice. **Executed over the ten
in-scope built pages:**

| Mutation | min units | max | MAX≤350 | MIN≥40 | LANDMARK | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| honest | 57 | 309 | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | green |
| **drop `head:meta:*`** | 54 | 309 | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | 🔴 **ALL FOUR GREEN** |
| **drop all `head`** | 53 | 309 | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | 🔴 **ALL FOUR GREEN** |
| **drop `jsonld:*`** | 55 | 309 | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | 🔴 **ALL FOUR GREEN** |
| **drop `attr`** | 50 | 309 | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | 🔴 **ALL FOUR GREEN** |
| **drop head + jsonld + attr** | 44 | 309 | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | 🔴 **ALL FOUR GREEN** |
| drop `body` | 13 | 222 | ✓ | ✗ | ✗ | red |

🔴 **Dropping `head:meta` and `jsonld` silently removes EXACTLY the two surfaces
tier A exists for and R8-3c amends.** The LANDMARK is a `body` landmark; **four
of the five block classes have no positive twin at all.**

**→ ONE LANDMARK PER BLOCK CLASS**, each a string that exists today and that
this ticket does not edit:

| Block class | Landmark |
| --- | --- |
| `body` | the footer sentence (R8-2c, unchanged) |
| `head:title` | 🆕 the page `<title>`'s own site suffix |
| `head:meta:description` | 🆕 **assert the block EXISTS and is non-empty on every page** — its text is what the ticket rewrites, so the twin is structural, not textual |
| `jsonld:*` | 🆕 assert **at least one** JSON-LD prose block per page carrying `@type` |
| `attr` | 🆕 a known `alt` string from the hero image |

⚠️ **This is R8-2c's own lesson applied to the thing R8-2c did not vary.** *A
mutation harness that varies one dimension proves nothing about the other.*

**Executed — every `1.5` in `src/lib/` that reaches rendered copy, with codepoints.** ⚠️ **`grep -rn "1\.5" src/lib/` returns SEVEN; the three omitted (`booking-email.ts:1132`, `booking-pricing.ts:87`, `:92`) are comments or arithmetic (R9-10 U16). *The first draft said "every 1.5" and listed four — and this table is what the third-spelling blocker rests on, so it says which four and why.*

| Site | Spelling | Char after `1.5` | Live to a customer? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `booking-copy.ts:597` `AFTER_HOURS_NOTE` | `1.5×` | U+00D7 | deleted by this ticket |
| `booking-copy.ts:551` | `1.5x` | U+0078 | **a comment** |
| `booking-email.ts:1167` | `1.5x` | U+0078 | email |
| 🔴 **`booking-copy.ts:461` `FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT[1]`** | **`1.5 times`** | **U+0020** | 🔴 **YES — verified live in `dist/client/book/index.html`** |

**R8-9's `W53-30` says *"both spellings, `1.5×` and `1.5x`"*.** The live web
spelling is neither. 🔴 **R8-8 ROW 18 — *"restore the weekend sentence to
`ASSESSMENT_TERMS`"* — restores `FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT[1]`, which says `1.5 times`,
so it STAYS GREEN for the second consecutive revision**, now for a reason
different from the corpus defect R8-1 believed it had fixed.
*(BK-55's G3 does carry `1.5 times`; R7-5 and R8-9 then described W53-30 as
"both spellings" and an implementer writing `[x×]` from that sentence is
correct to the words and wrong to the copy.)*

**→ `W53-30`'s multiplier shape is `/1\.5\s*(?:[x×]|times)\b/i` — THREE
spellings, named.**

### 🔴 R9-5a · THE POSITIVE CONTROL RULE — the structural answer to B2, N8, S1, S7, S8

> **Every ban carries a POSITIVE CONTROL: a string that exists in the repo today
> and that the ban MUST match. The control is asserted, and it is asserted
> BEFORE the ban's negative arm.**

**A ban whose shapes cannot see the live copy is a ban that has never been
aimed.** This is CLAUDE.md's negative-assertion trap with a cheap standing
remedy, and it catches every instance this ticket has paid for:

| Ban | Positive control, verified present today |
| --- | --- |
| `W53-30` weekend | `FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT[1]` — *"charged at **1.5 times** the weekday price"* |
| `MONEY_PROMISE` | the live eyebrow — *"**Credited in full** when you go ahead"* (N8) |
| `FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES` | `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0]` **and** `llms.txt.ts:54`'s claim |
| `AMOUNT_SHAPES` | `FEE_TERMS_ITEMS`'s `$399` **before** the copy lands; after it, the `insurance-claims` `$500` string used as a fixture, never as a scanned file |
| `BANNED_SHAPES` (ics) | each of the seven, against the draft it is shaped for |

⚠️ **The control runs against a FIXTURE STRING, not against the scanned corpus**
— otherwise it goes red the moment the ticket succeeds. **This is the twin
discipline the Gate Design Lesson names, applied to the SHAPE rather than to the
surface.**

## R9-7 · THE ADVERSARY'S SIX NITS

| # | Nit | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | R8-2c's *"400 misses the no-split mutation by 35 characters"* is **arithmetically wrong against its own table** — that mutation maxes at 438, and `maxLen <= 400` catches it. *"500 misses three"* is correct | ✅ **CORRECT, and it was half the justification for pinning 350.** **The window `(273, 438]` is unchanged and 350 remains the pick** — for the surviving reason: 28% headroom over the honest max. *A number defended by a wrong argument and a right one is still only as good as the right one* |
| **2** | R8-8 row 23's *"caught by nothing else"* — `MIN_UNITS` also catches the body drop (14 units) | ✅ **CORRECT.** Row 23's justification is now **B4's four block drops**, which `MIN_UNITS` does **not** catch |
| **3** | 🔴 **An independent implementation gives honest HTML max 309, not 273** (min units 57 matches exactly). **`MAX_UNIT` is extractor-implementation-sensitive and the ticket ships no reference implementation** | 🔴 **THE SHARPEST NIT.** 309 is still inside `(273, 438]` and 350 still holds — **but two honest implementations differ by 36 characters, and the next implementer's will differ again.** → **THE TICKET SHIPS THE EXTRACTOR AS CODE, not as prose**, and the constants are re-measured against **that** implementation and pinned with it. *A threshold measured against an implementation nobody can reproduce is not a threshold* |
| **4** | `BANNED_SHAPES[4]` misses `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NOTE`'s *"The visit is free whichever you pick"* — shaped for `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0]`'s exact wording | ✅ **CORRECT → `/(?:the )?(?:on-site )?(?:assessment )?visit[^.<>]{0,30}?\bis free\b/i`, with both drafts as positive controls (R9-5a)** |
| **5** | `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[2]` matches no `BANNED` value and no `BANNED_SHAPE` — the one member with no coverage | ✅ **CORRECT and DELIBERATE, now recorded:** `[2]` is the decision-20 sentence (*findings in person, quote follows*). It is **true on a cancellation email** and is not assessment-terms money copy. **Recorded so it is not "fixed" into the ban** |
| **6** | R8-2b's block spec has no rule for HTML comments in either direction | ✅ **FIXED — S2** |

| **A-R9-2** | 🆕 A block boundary is **format-defined** — a tag in HTML, a line in Markdown — and the self-checks are **per corpus class** | A threshold measured on HTML and applied to `llms.txt` is red on arrival on 3 of 4 checks (B1) |
| **A-R9-3** | 🆕 Coupling units are built **per member**; `join` is forbidden by name | Two revisions asserted a join was safe; it was measured unsafe twice (B5) |
| **A-R9-4** | 🆕 **Tier A is a property of the BLOCK, not a list of files** | Item 6 described a property; revision 8 implemented its two examples (S4) |
| **A-R9-5** | 🆕 **The extractor ships as CODE with the ticket**, and its constants are measured against that implementation | Two honest implementations differ by 36 characters (nit 3) |
| **A-R9-6** | 🆕 The blog's built pages are **out of scope and unowned** — a named gap, not a closed one | Client-authored, unbounded; gating it puts a copy gate on every future post (S10) |

# 🟧 REVISION 8 — 2026-09-06. ⛔ **SUPERSEDED BY REVISION 9. NOT THE SPEC.**

> 🔴 **Read revision 9 first.** Five of this revision's designs were measured
> broken, all executed: the `dist/` self-checks are **red on arrival on
> `llms.txt`** (R9-1), the coupling unit's `join` **welds members** (R9-2), the
> amount ban **cannot see a constant value** and dropped two ordered shapes
> (R9-3), the extractor has a positive twin for **one block class in five**
> (R9-4), and `W53-30` bans two spellings while the live one is a **third**
> (R9-5). **Everything not contradicted by R9-0's absorption table stands**, and
> R8-7, R8-8, R8-9 and R8-10 stand entire.

Written against the traceability audit (**R7-A**, 11🔴/13🟠) and the adversarial
pre-read (**R7-B**, 10🔴/8 SF/11 nits), both transcribed in full above rather
than cited as a count — **R7-A's own finding 🔴2, discharged for this round by
this sentence and by R8-0's table.**

**Two of R7-B's blockers are design-level and everything else in this revision
follows from them:** the bans were scoped to a corpus that does not contain the
copy they ban (**B2**), and the sentence rule that replaced the whole-file ban is
weaker than what it replaced on the one surface the customer actually reads
(**B4**). **A third, B1, is worse than either: the approved copy is red on
arrival under revision 7's own rule.**

## R8-1 · 🔴 B2 — THE BANS SCAN A CORPUS THAT DOES NOT CONTAIN THE COPY

**R7-B's finding, verified independently and then found to be *half* the defect.**

R7-2 scopes `MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES` and R7-5 scopes W30 to `WEB_CLAIM_SURFACES` —
`book.astro`, `ContactSection.astro`, `BookingForm.svelte`, `llms.txt.ts`,
`[service].astro`. **Verified, per file:**

```
book.astro:141           {FEE_TERMS_ITEMS.map((item) => <li>{item}</li>)}
book.astro:143,146       {FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT.map(...)}  {FEE_TERMS_REFUND.map(...)}
ContactSection.astro:349,351,354   the same three, same shape
BookingForm.svelte:1115,1119,1122  {#each FEE_TERMS_ITEMS as item} …
[service].astro          no FEE_TERMS_* reference of any kind
```

**A scan of those files sees identifier NAMES, never the sentences.** Restoring
*"…and we refund you in full."* into a constant renders on all three pages and
matches nothing — R7-B measured red-first rows **15 and 18 both GREEN**. It is
**R6-1's own finding**, which measured this property fatal in `verify-cutover.ts`
and wrote a `src/` value import to fix it, **reproduced one revision later inside
the fix for it.**

🔴 **AND THE CONVERSE, WHICH R7-B DID NOT STATE — the fix as R7-B proposes it
(*"run the bans over `TERMS_TEXT`"*) is blind in the other direction.**
Verified, live today:

| Prose, in a template, in no constant | Money promise? |
| --- | --- |
| `book.astro:37` meta description — *"the fee is **credited back in full** when you go ahead with the work"* | **YES**, and it is in **frontmatter** (B10) |
| `llms.txt.ts:54` — *"The assessment fee is **credited in full against the final invoice**"* | **YES** |

**Neither is in any constant, so neither is in `TERMS_TEXT`.** A ban over
`TERMS_TEXT` alone ships both. **Each corpus is blind to exactly what the other
one holds.**

### → The corpus is the UNION, it is built ONCE, and every web-side ban uses it

**`WEB_PROSE` — one definition, three consumers (coupling, money-promise,
weekend), replacing three ad-hoc scopes:**

1. **`TERMS_TEXT`** — the imported VALUES, per R6-3a, unchanged. *What the
   constants say.*
2. **`TEMPLATE_PROSE`** — the five `WEB_CLAIM_SURFACES` files, **whole file
   through `stripForUse`, frontmatter included** (B10; the ban this replaces at
   `verify-cutover.ts:470-476` already reads the whole file, and narrowing to
   `templateOf` is how the new meta description landed in its own replacement's
   blind spot). *What the templates say in their own words.*
3. **`DIST_TEXT`** — the rendered text of the built pages, per R8-2. *What the
   customer reads, after both of the above have been through a build.*

⚠️ **Each of the three asserts it is NON-EMPTY and within a pre-registered size
range, individually.** A union whose halves can silently empty is the
scanned-file-count defect (R6-4 A9b) one level up: `WEB_PROSE.length > 0` is
satisfied by any one of the three.

⚠️ **Why `DIST_TEXT` does not make 1 and 2 redundant, stated so it is not
"simplified" away later:** `llms.txt` is not HTML and is not in `bundles`;
`[service].astro` renders four pages; and a source-arm failure names the FILE
while a dist-arm failure names a built artifact nobody edits. **1 and 2 locate
the defect; 3 proves the build did not reintroduce it.**

⚠️ **`stripForUse`'s `//` rule is `/(^|[^:])\/\/[^\n]*/g`** — deliberately, so it
does not eat `https://`. `TEMPLATE_PROSE` uses it. It does **not** run on
`DIST_TEXT`, where every character is real (R6-3e's binding rule, restated
because this revision adds a third corpus and the rule was written for two).

🔴 **B3 dissolves here, in both of its directions.** R7-2's *"per-constant over
`booking-copy.ts`"* scoping is what made `MONEY_PROMISE` fire on four
KEEP-UNCHANGED email constants (`REFUND_TIMING_LINE`, `REFUNDED_HEADING`,
`refundedAmountLine()`, `PAYMENT_ATTENTION_RULE`) while missing `REFUNDED_LEAD`
and `CANCELLED_REFUNDED_LEAD` two lines away. **Under `WEB_PROSE` the question
does not arise: none of the six is in `TERMS_TEXT`, none renders on a web
surface, and the ban is scoped by WHAT REACHES A CUSTOMER'S SCREEN rather than
by which file a constant happens to live in.** *This is the same move R6-3a made
when it withdrew the exempt list, and it is worth naming as the pattern: an
exemption is how a ban dies quietly; a corpus defined by reachability needs
none.*
## R8-2 · 🔴 B4 — WHAT THE `dist/` ARM ASSERTS. **Measured, and the fix is validated 8/8**

### 2a · The defect, reproduced — and R7-B's description corrected

**The numbers are exact.** `/(?<=[.!?])\s+/` over the raw built files:

| File | "sentences" | **max** | p50 | p90 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `book/index.html` | **38** | **18,299** | 244 | 1,576 |
| **`index.html`** | 87 | 🔴 **21,181** | 398 | 4,080 |
| **`contact/index.html`** | 35 | 🔴 **20,060** | 481 | 4,467 |
| the four `[service]` pages | 56–67 | 18,370–18,466 | 234–438 | ~1,500 |
| `llms.txt` | 38 | **397** | 106 | 193 |

⚠️ **R7-B measured the LEAST-BAD page.** The homepage and `/contact/` are worse,
and both carry the terms box.

⚠️ 🔴 **AND ONE CLAUSE OF R7-B's FINDING IS FALSE — do not transcribe it.** R7-B
says the blob spans *"the JSON-LD through the whole SSR'd `<astro-island>` —
**which is where the terms box renders**."* **Verified: the 18,299-character unit
occupies offsets 3,696–21,995 and does NOT contain the terms box.**
*"Assessment terms"* is at offset **27,702**, inside a separate **244-character**
unit. **The vacuity is real and was reproduced directly** (arm b2 below) — but it
is not the mechanism R7-B describes, and a reviewer who checks the stated
mechanism will find it false and may discard the finding with it.
⚠️ **`llms.txt` was never affected** — it is plain text, so periods are followed
by whitespace and its max unit is 397. **The defect is HTML-only**, which is why
a source-arm measurement could not have found it.

### 2b · → THE `dist/` ARM ASSERTS OVER RENDERED TEXT, IN BLOCKS

```
DIST_TEXT(file) = ordered BLOCKS, each split into units by /(?<=[.!?])\s+/:
    head:title · head:meta:description · head:meta:og:description
  · one block per JSON-LD prose string   (keys: description, headline, text,
      articleBody, abstract, reviewBody, caption, slogan, answerText,
      alternativeHeadline, disambiguatingDescription, about — NEVER areaServed or @id)
  · attr  (alt, aria-label, title, placeholder)
  · body  (<script>/<style>/<template> dropped whole; every remaining tag replaced
      by a space; entities decoded; whitespace collapsed; the space that tag-stripping
      inserts BEFORE punctuation removed)
```

🔴 **Blocks, not one string — adjacency NEVER crosses a block edge.** A search
snippet renders the meta description with no page around it to qualify the claim;
that is the argument `verify-cutover.ts:1188-1193` already makes about `llms.txt`,
applied to the `<head>`. *(And it is tier A of R8-3b, which is why the two
sections agree by construction rather than by coincidence.)*

⚠️ 🔴 **A BLOCK-LEVEL TAG EMITS A BOUNDARY SENTINEL; A SOURCE NEWLINE MUST NOT.**
Measured defect in the first version of this extractor: using `\n` as the block
mark and preserving literal newlines made `/book/`'s HTML line-wrap
(*"…what it takes to\nput it right."*) a **false unit boundary**, which shifts a
qualifier from *"the next sentence"* to *"two sentences later"* and manufactures a
**false RED**. **Found and fixed before it was specified — recorded because the
next implementer will reach for `\n`.**

**Result: `/book/`'s max unit falls from 18,299 to 193 — 95×.** Eight-file
corpus **n = 1,351, p50 26, p90 107, p99 189, max 222.** Over all **18** built
pages: max **273**, min units/file **57**.

### 2c · 🔴 THE EXTRACTOR ASSERTS THAT IT DID NOT SILENTLY FAIL

**B4's failure mode was that the splitter produced an 18 KB unit and nothing
noticed. A max-length guard is necessary and NOT sufficient** — measured against
seven mutations:

| Mutation of the extractor | max unit | units/file | landmark |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **honest control** | **273** | **57** | ✅ |
| block tags stop emitting a boundary | 466 | 22 | ✅ |
| `<script>` contents kept | 2,931 | 58 | ✅ |
| tag-stripping regex matches nothing | 466 | 22 | ✅ |
| sentence split removed | **438** | 33 | ✅ |
| 🔴 **`body` block dropped entirely** | **222 — GREEN** | 14 | ❌ **missing** |
| whitespace not collapsed | 275 | 57 | ✅ |

**→ Four assertions, per file. Each catches something the others do not:**

```ts
// Pinned against the measured distribution (2026-09-06, dist of 2026-09-05):
// 18 built pages, 1,351 units over the eight pinned — p50 26, p90 107, p99 189,
// max 222; max over ALL built pages 273; min units/file 57. The window for
// MAX_UNIT is (273, 438]: the honest max on one side, the WEAKEST detectable
// break on the other. 400 misses the no-split mutation by 35 characters; 500
// misses three. This number is not arbitrary and must be re-measured, not
// nudged, after any layout or dependency change.
const MAX_UNIT  = 350;
const MIN_UNITS = 40;    // honest floor 57; two mutations -> 22, one -> 14
const LANDMARK  = 'Specialists answer every call — no voicemail, no hold music, no call centres.';

check(maxLen <= MAX_UNIT,   `${file}: a ${maxLen}-character unit — the splitter has stopped seeing sentence boundaries, so "same sentence or the next" now means "somewhere in this region". Every finding below this line is unsound until it is fixed.`);
check(units >= MIN_UNITS,   `${file}: only ${units} readable units — the corpus has collapsed, so a green coupling pin here is evidence of nothing.`);
check(bodyUnits.includes(LANDMARK), `${file}: the extractor did not recover the footer sentence as a whole unit — without this control, "no unqualified free claim" may mean "no body text was read at all".`);
check(residue.length === 0, `${file}: ${residue.length} HTML entities survived decoding — copy this extractor cannot read, and a claim inside it would pass unseen.`);
```

🔴 **The LANDMARK is the one that matters**, and it is the positive twin this
ticket keeps re-learning it needs: **dropping the entire body block leaves the
max at 222 — comfortably green.** ~~and is caught by nothing else~~
⛔ **STRUCK IN PLACE 2026-09-06 (R9-7 nit 2 / R9-10 U13): `MIN_UNITS` DOES catch
the body drop** (14 units), as this section's own comment says three lines up.
🔴 **The LANDMARK's real justification is R9-4: it is the only check that catches
dropping `head:meta`, `jsonld` or `attr`, none of which move either number.** It matches
exactly on all 17 built HTML pages.

### 2d · Executed adversarially — 8 of 8, each asserting the break LANDED first

| Arm | Scenario | Expect | Extractor | The naive rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a | claim + qualifier, one rendered sentence | GREEN | ✅ GREEN | GREEN |
| b | qualifier ~18,000 chars away | RED | ✅ RED | RED |
| **b2** | 🔴 **both inside the 18,299-char naive unit** | RED | ✅ **RED** | 🔴 **GREEN — B4 reproduced** |
| c | qualifier in the immediately following sentence | GREEN | ✅ GREEN | GREEN |
| d | qualifier two sentences later | RED | ✅ RED | RED |
| **e** | qualifier split by inline tags — `travel <span>fee</span> may apply` | GREEN | ✅ **GREEN** | 🔴 **RED — a false alarm on copy that reads perfectly** |
| f | unqualified claim in JSON-LD `description` | RED | ✅ RED | RED |
| g | untouched control, finding count unchanged | baseline | ✅ baseline | — |

**Arms b2 and e are what justify the work:** b2 is B4's exact vacuity, and e is a
**false alarm the naive rule raises today** — the direction nobody was looking.

### 2e · JSON-LD is IN scope, and `[^.<>]` does NOT change

- **JSON-LD in scope.** Its `description` on this build is byte-identical to
  `<meta name="description">`, `og:description` and `twitter:description`;
  excluding it while pinning the meta description would be arbitrary, Google
  renders it standalone, and **this build's descriptions still say *"the fee is
  credited back in full"***. The key list is narrow so `areaServed` city names and
  `@id` URLs never enter the corpus.
- 🔴 **DO NOT widen `[^.<>]` for the dist arm.** Measured: **zero behaviour
  differences** between `[^.<>]` and `[^.]` across all 1,351 units — only one
  unit in the whole corpus still contains an angle bracket. **But the widening is
  real if the shapes run over the CONCATENATED text**, executed:
  ```
  <nav><a>Toll free</a><a>Careers</a></nav><h2>Assessment tiers</h2>
    raw HTML, shape 1:              false
    whole-document extracted text:  TRUE   <-- false positive
    per-unit extracted text:        false  <-- correct
  ```
  **→ The requirement is a MATCHING DISCIPLINE, not a regex edit: test one unit
  at a time, never a joined document — and say so at the regex,** because a
  reader of `[^.<>]` will assume it bounds elements and after extraction it does
  not.

### 2f · Baseline — the pin has teeth on the real build

Over today's pre-cutover `dist/`, the arm reports **6 unqualified free claims**
that the naive rule buries: *"Nothing is charged when you send your request."*
(`/book/`, `/`) and *"About 30 minutes, no obligation."* (all four service
pages). ✅ **This corroborates R7-3c's red-on-arrival ordering claim and R7-B's
independent count of exactly six** — two measurements, agreeing, on the number
that orders the copy before the pin.

### 2g · 🔴 R0 — WHAT THE `dist/` HTML ARM CANNOT SEE, AND WHY IT NEEDS A THIRD CORPUS

**Measured: `RECEIVED_EMAILED_LINE`'s text appears in ZERO built HTML files and
in one `_astro/*.js` chunk.** Copy that exists only after hydration is invisible
to any dist-HTML extractor — **and that is precisely the BK-36 island
fallback-card surface named in CLAUDE.md's copy-inventory trap.**

**→ Two consequences, both binding:**
1. **The `dist/` arm must NOT be described as covering the island's post-submit
   states.** Say what it does not cover, in its comment.
2. ✅ **The gap is already closed at SOURCE by `WEB_CONSTANTS` (R8-4b)** — the
   hydration-only copy lives in `booking-copy.ts` constants that
   `BookingConfirmation.svelte` imports, and the closure assertion puts them in
   `WEB_PROSE`. **On `dist/`, `termsBundles` is the pattern for a JS-chunk corpus
   and `verify-cutover.ts` already builds it.** *This is the clearest case yet
   for why `WEB_PROSE` has three halves and not one.*

⚠️ **Residual weaknesses, recorded UNFIXED rather than left to be discovered:**
a claim hidden by an inline tag **inside a word** (`is fr<span>ee</span>.`)
survives — the fix welds `<li>free</li><li>Assessment` into `freeAssessment` and
is worse; a claim in an `<astro-island props="{…}">` JSON attribute (**not
hypothetical — `index.html` already ships `beforeAlt`/`afterAlt` prose that
way**); a claim in CSS `content:`; a claim in a `data-*` attribute; a claim under
a JSON-LD key outside the list. **And one FALSE-RED source: abbreviations.**
*"…free, i.e. no charge. Approx. 30 min. A travel fee may apply."* over-splits and
fires. **Safe direction, but it will block a gate on legitimate copy and the
tempting fix is to widen the adjacency window — which is B4 all over again.
Fix the copy, never the window.**
⚠️ **Coverage gap named rather than closed:** the pinned list is 8 files; **10
further built pages exist, including `/book/received/` and `/book/confirmed/`
— the two pages a customer sees AFTER the free visit is promised.** Run clean
today, verified over all 18. **→ They join `DIST_TEXT`'s enumerated scope
(R8-1).** ⛔ ~~*the eleven becomes thirteen*~~ **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-06 (R8-10 T13):
`received` and `confirmed` were already INSIDE the eleven. The scope is ELEVEN
files plus `termsBundles`, enumerated at R8-10c** — *a scope described by a
number three different ways is the hazard A-R8-1 exists to prevent, committed by
A-R8-1's own section.*
## R8-3 · 🔴 B1 — THE APPROVED COPY IS RED ON ARRIVAL, AND THE RULE IS LOOSER THAN THE ONE IT IMPLEMENTS

### 3a · A-R7-1's basis is FALSE, and the rule it justified is a loosening

**A-R7-1 records the basis:** *"Decision 17a says **same sentence**."*
🔴 **Decision 17a says no such thing.** Its full text (this file, decision 17a)
is about W22's ownership and about disclosing that a fee *exists*; the phrase
*"same sentence"* appears nowhere in decision 17, 17a or 23. **The citation was
invented in the direction of the conclusion.**

**The requirement is real; it comes from somewhere else, and it is STRICTER than
what R7-3a wrote.** Plan review round 3B, item 6, verbatim in this file:

> *"**"free" survives unqualified in the two places most likely to be quoted OUT
> of context** — `llms.txt` and the eyebrow. §1's coupling pin is per-surface, so
> an LLM extract or a SERP snippet passes the pin and drops the qualifier.* →
> **bind claim and qualifier in the SAME SENTENCE**, not merely the same file."

**R7-3a implemented that as *"in that sentence or the one immediately following
it"* — which is defeated by the exact attack the requirement was written
against.** A snippet takes one sentence. *A rule rewrite that loosens its own
predicate and keeps citing the old rationale is CLAUDE.md's rule-rewrite trap;
R7-A caught the same shape one section over and this one survived it.*

### 3b · → THE RULE, IN THREE TIERS. The strictness is chosen by HOW THE SURFACE IS CONSUMED

| Tier | Surfaces | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A — extract surfaces** | `llms.txt`, the `book.astro` meta description **and its JSON-LD twin at `:49`**, the eyebrow (`book.astro:94`, `ContactSection.astro:287`) | **SAME SENTENCE. No next-sentence relief.** These surfaces exist to be quoted with no page around them — round 3B item 6's rationale, applied to exactly the surfaces it named |
| **B — rendered surfaces** | `TERMS_TEXT`, `[service].astro:79`, everything else in `WEB_PROSE` | **Same sentence, or the immediately following one within the same exported symbol** |
| **C — overriding, everywhere** | any sentence matching **both** a free shape and an obligation-release shape (`FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES[3]`, `[4]`) | **SAME SENTENCE, no relief, in tier B too.** This is **decision 23 constraint 1** — *"it must NEVER sit next to a bare 'free' without the travel-fee qualifier"* — which the same-or-next rule **cannot express**, and which R7-3a therefore silently dropped |

🔴 **The unit for *"the immediately following sentence"* is the exported
SYMBOL, its members joined in render order — not the array element.** B1 is
right that R7-3 left this undefined, and the reading is not a coin-flip:
**executed, the element reading is RED on `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0]`** — *"The
on-site assessment visit is free."*, the most important claim on the site, whose
qualifier deliberately lives in `[1]`. **The element reading forbids the copy
decision 26 approved and R6-2 designed. The symbol reading is the one that
matches what a customer reads: a `<ul>` of consecutive sentences.**
⚠️ **Join members with `'\n'`, not `' '`** (R7-3b, restated because it is now
load-bearing for a different reason): the newline keeps the split honest at
member boundaries and cannot manufacture a claim across one.

### 3c · 🔴 THE AMEND LIST — **DERIVED BY EXECUTION, NOT FROM THE WORD "FREE"**

**B1's second half is the method complaint, and it is the one that matters:
R7-3b derived its list by looking for *"free"* and named two sentences. Run the
ten `FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES` over every approved draft and the list is different in
both directions.** Executed against R6-2's copy as approved:

| Surface | Sentence | Shapes | Verdict | Why R7-3b missed it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[3]` | *"There is no obligation to go ahead."* | `[3]` | 🔴 **RED**, both readings | **The sentence does not contain the word *"free"*.** It is an obligation-release, which decision 23 made a first-class subject of the pin and R7-3b's search could not see |
| `book.astro:36-37` meta description **+ JSON-LD `:49`** | *"Free on-site restoration assessment in Edmonton — about 30 minutes, no obligation."* | `[0,3]` | 🔴 **RED** — tier A **and** tier C | **Not found by B1 either.** The qualifier is in the *next* sentence, which R7-3a's own rule permits and both stricter tiers forbid. *It is also plan-review round 3B item 7, which said the qualifier is "the truncated tail" and was never discharged* |
| `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NOTE` | *"The visit is free whichever you pick — a travel fee may apply further out."* | `[1]` | ✅ **green** | R7-3b amended it; the amendment is correct and **stands** |
| `FEE_TERMS_ITEMS[0]` | bare *"**Free.**"* dropped | — | ✅ **no claim remains** | R7-3b's second amendment is correct and **stands** |
| `[service].astro:79` | *"About 30 minutes, no obligation."* | `[3]` | ✅ **green** (tier B; qualifier in the next sentence, and no free claim shares the sentence so tier C does not fire) | — |
| eyebrow | *"Free on-site assessment — travel fee may apply further out"* | `[0]` | ✅ **green** (tier A) | — |
| `llms.txt.ts:54` | one sentence carrying claim **and** qualifier | `[1]` | ✅ **green** (tier A) | R6-2 got this one right deliberately |
| `FEE_TERMS_ACK_LABEL` | — | **none** | ✅ **not a subject** | ⚠️ **Worth stating:** it carries the qualifier and makes no claim. **That is why red-first row 1 was green under R6's blob** — the label donated a qualifier to a claim seven sentences away. Per-sentence, it can no longer do so |

**→ TWO drafts change. Both are decision-17a/23 compliance corrections in the
class A-R7-3 already established, not taste changes to decision 26's approval:**

- **`ASSESSMENT_TERMS[3]`** → *"There is no obligation to go ahead: the visit
  itself costs you nothing beyond a travel fee, if one applies to your address."*
  ✅ Executed green under tiers B **and** C. ⚠️ It now matches shape `[8]`
  (*"costs you nothing"*) as well as `[3]` — **deliberately**: it carries its own
  qualifier, so more shapes matching it is more coverage, not more fragility.
- **`book.astro:36-37` + `:49`** → *"Free on-site restoration assessment in
  Edmonton — about 30 minutes, no obligation, though a travel fee may apply
  further out. We say the amount up front."*
  ✅ Executed green under tiers A and C; **153 characters.**
  🔴 **And the LENGTH pin becomes a RANGE, not `=== 148`** — `>= 120` (a gutted
  description still describes the page) and `<= 155` (the SERP truncation bound,
  which is the pin's actual reason). **R6-2 pinned a scalar; R7-7 established for
  the assertion count that a scalar invites correction over investigation, and a
  length pin is the same object.** *R6-2's own parenthetical — "the ticket said
  151 twice" — is that failure already happening once.*

### 3d · The positive twin, corrected — N6

**N6, verified:** the twin *"at least one sentence in the corpus matches a free
shape"* is satisfiable by `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NOTE` **alone**, so deleting every
free claim from `ASSESSMENT_TERMS` leaves twin and coupling **both green** — the
terms box could stop claiming the visit is free at all and nothing would fire.
**→ The twin is PER SYMBOL, not per corpus:** `ASSESSMENT_TERMS` **must** contain
at least one free claim, and so must the eyebrow's line, `llms.txt` and the
`[service].astro` line — **each asserted separately, with its own failure
message naming the symbol.** A corpus-wide twin is the `offenders.length === 0`
defect (R6-4 A9b) in the positive direction.
## R8-4 · `MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES` — the shapes, and the closure that stops the scope going stale

### 4a · 🔴 N8, executed: the ban misses the claim that is LIVE ON THE SITE TODAY

`MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES[1]` is
`/\bcredit(?:ed|s)?\b[^.<>]{0,40}?\b(?:invoice|final bill|against|back)\b/i` —
it requires a noun after *"credited"*. **Verified against the two live
eyebrows, `book.astro:94` and `ContactSection.astro:287`:**

```
MISS    "Credited in full when you go ahead"          <- LIVE ON BOTH PAGES TODAY
MATCH   "The assessment fee is credited in full against the final invoice"
MATCH   "the fee is credited back in full when you go ahead with the work"
```

**So the one money promise a reverting hand is most likely to restore — the
sentence that is on the site right now — is the one the ban cannot see.** *This
is BK-48's negative-assertion trap exactly: the shape was derived from the
`FEE_TERMS_CREDIT` and `llms.txt` spellings, which both happen to carry a noun,
and the site's third spelling carries none.*

**→ Under decision 17 there is nothing to credit and no qualified form is true
(A-R7-2), so the bare participle is the ban:**

```ts
const MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES = [
  /\brefunds?(?:ed|able|ing)?\b/i,
  /\bcredited\b/i,                                   // 🔴 N8 — bare; the live eyebrow carries no noun
  /\bcredits?\b[^.<>]{0,40}?\b(?:invoice|bill|against|back|balance|what you owe)\b/i,
  /\bcomes?\s+off\b[^.<>]{0,30}?\b(?:invoice|bill|total|what you owe)\b/i,
  /\b(?:applied|goes?|put)\s+(?:towards?|toward|against)\b[^.<>]{0,30}?\b(?:invoice|bill|total|job|work)\b/i,
  /\btaken\s+off\b[^.<>]{0,30}?\b(?:invoice|bill|what you owe)\b/i,
  /\breimburses?(?:d|ment)?\b/i,
  /\bmoney[\s-]?back\b/i,
  /\breturn(?:ed|ing)?\s+(?:it\s+)?to\s+your\s+(?:card|account)\b/i,
  /\bnon[\s-]?(?:refundable|returnable)\b/i,
  /\bdeposits?\b/i,
  /\bholding\s+(?:payment|fee|deposit)\b/i,
];
```
**SF2's seven spellings are all covered above** (*return it to your card*,
*applied to your bill*, *toward your final invoice*, *taken off what you owe*,
*reimburse*, *non-returnable*, *a holding payment up front*). ⚠️ **`credit card`
is NOT matched** — `/\bcredited\b/` and the noun-bearing form both exclude it;
verified rather than assumed, because a false positive here is what makes the
next editor delete the arm (SF3's failure mode).

⚠️ **`FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES[6]` gains `nothing has been charged` (N7).** Verified: it
is the site's dominant spelling — `booking-copy.ts:705`, `:785`, `:829` — and
the existing alternation (`nothing is charged|not charged|…`) matches none of
them. **It changes no verdict in this ticket** (all three are email lifecycle
constants, out of `WEB_PROSE`) **and that is exactly why it goes in now**: a
shape must be written for the category, not for the strings that happen to be in
scope on the day.

### 4b · 🔴 THE CLOSURE — 🟠14, B10, 🟠23, R7-3c and R7-4 are ONE defect, five times

Five findings across two rounds say the same thing: **a scope was enumerated by
hand and a surface was left out of it.**

| Finding | The surface that was missed |
| --- | --- |
| R7-3c | the three template surfaces the ticket edits |
| R7-4 | `llms.txt`, the four `[service]` pages, `contact/index.html` |
| B10 | `book.astro`'s frontmatter meta description |
| 🟠23 | `/contact/` in the coupling pin's `dist/` arm |
| 🟠14 | **`book/payment-cancelled.astro`, in NEITHER scope** — and BK-53 rewrites `PAYMENT_CANCELLED_LEAD`, so freshly written copy ships on a fully static public route with no coupling pin and no money ban |

**Enumerating a sixth time is the same move that failed five times.** → **Assert
the scope's CLOSURE instead:**

> **`WEB_CONSTANTS` — every `booking-copy` symbol imported by a prerendered page
> under `src/pages/`, or by any component such a page renders — is a
> PRE-REGISTERED LIST, and the gate asserts the discovered set EQUALS it.**

Adding a page, or an import to an existing one, then fails the build with a
message naming the new symbol, instead of silently widening the copy that no pin
reads. **Verified, the discovered set today:**

```
book.astro                       FEE_TERMS_* (the terms box), ASSESSMENT_TIER_*, …
book/payment-cancelled.astro     PAYMENT_CANCELLED_HEADING, _HELP_LINE, _LEAD      🔴 in no scope today
BookingConfirmation.svelte       RECEIVED_*, HAVE_READY_*, EMAILED_LINE, …          (via book/received.astro)
ContactSection.astro             the same terms symbols as book.astro               (via index.astro and contact.astro)
BookingForm.svelte               the same terms symbols
```

**`WEB_PROSE`'s constant half is the VALUES of `WEB_CONSTANTS`**, of which
`TERMS_TEXT` is a subset. The coupling pin's tier-B rule, `MONEY_PROMISE` and
W30 all run over it.

⚠️ **This is a NEW assertion over copy nobody has scanned before, so its colour
on arrival is UNKNOWN and must be MEASURED, not predicted.** *(BK-48: a
configuration change is not evidence of its own effect.)* **→ Red-first row 20
and a pre-registration slot; if it is red on arrival, the offending copy is
either a real find or a shape defect, and the ticket says which before it
loosens anything.** Reviewed prediction, recorded so a wrong one is visible:
`PAYMENT_CANCELLED_*` and `RECEIVED_*` make no free claim and no money promise
under R6-2's redrafts, so **green** is expected.
## R8-5 · `WEB_AMOUNT_BAN` — B9, B10, SF3, SF4

**Every figure below was re-measured.** ⚠️ 🔴 **AND ONE OF THOSE RE-MEASUREMENTS
WAS WRONG.** R8-5a's first draft refuted **B9**, whose count is in fact exact;
the refutation is withdrawn in place below and recorded at **R8-10 T1**. **B10,
SF4 and B9 all stand as written by R7-B. Only SF3's numbers are wrong**, and
that one *is* an argument for weakening a ban, so it is the one to check hardest.

🔴 **The lesson is this revision's, not a validator's:** *a refutation is a
measurement and gets the same standard as the claim it refutes.* R8-5a's first
draft reached its conclusion by measuring one plausible reading of *"the
four-digit year excluded by name"* and concluding **no** reading produced the
number. **Enumerate the readings, or do not refute.**

### 5a · 🔴 B9 — the count is wrong, and so is the conclusion drawn from it

🔴🔴 **THIS TABLE'S FIRST ROW WAS ITSELF FALSE AND IS WITHDRAWN — see R8-10 T1.**
**B9's count is EXACT and its item list is internally consistent.** Re-measured
independently, three ways:

| Pipeline | Count | Composition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| raw, no carve-outs | 64 | — |
| `stripForUse` only | 49 | — |
| `stripForUse` + phone | 41 | `500:27 2000:7 200:2 1024:2 100:1 123:1 429:1` |
| `stripForUse` + phone + **`\d{4}`** | 32 | drops `1024`×2 **and `2000`×7** |
| 🔴 **`stripForUse` + phone + `(19\|20)\d{2}`** — *"the four-digit year excluded by name"* | ✅ **34** | **`500:27 200:2 1024:2 100:1 123:1 429:1`** |

**That last composition is B9's named list, item for item** — `text-red-500` ×27,
`10 * 1024 * 1024`, an HTTP `429`, `"123 Maple St"`. **A year carve-out written
as `(19|20)\d{2}` removes the seven `xmlns=…2000`s, which is exactly the
difference between 41 and 34.** *The first draft of this section measured a
literal LIST of years instead, which does not contain `2000`, got 41, and
concluded no combination reached 34.*
⚠️ **And the "inconsistent item list" charge was a MISATTRIBUTION:** `xmlns=…2000`
is **N11's** subject, not B9's. **B9 never mentions it.**

| R7-B's other B9 claims | Measured |
| --- | --- |
| *"`text-red-500` ×27"* | ✅ **exact** |
| *"an HTTP 429"* | ⚠️ 3 raw, **1** survives `stripForUse`; the other two are comments |
| *"**every naive fix deletes the coverage the shape exists for**"* | ✅ **TRUE OF NAIVE FIXES, and the remedy below is not one.** Every *shape* or *carve-out* fix does delete coverage — measured. **A SCOPE fix does not:** green on arrival with one carve-out and red on every hazard. **B9 is a correct finding, correctly counted, and R8-5b is its answer** |

🔴 **AND THE CARVE-OUT R7-4 SPECIFIES BY NAME IS A HOLE THE SIZE OF THIS SITE'S
TOP TIER.** R7-4 says *"with the four-digit year … excluded by name"*. Executed:

| Planted copy | no carve-out | **`\d{4}` carve** | literal-year carve |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| *"normally 399."* | RED | RED | RED |
| *"normally 1199."* | RED | 🔴 **GREEN — MISS** | RED |
| *"normally 1200."* | RED | 🔴 **GREEN — MISS** | RED |
| *"normally 1,199."* | RED | RED | RED |

**`$1,199` is a real tier price and `1199` is a four-digit number.** A `\d{4}`
carve-out is *shaped for the thing it is trying to catch* — R7-4's own A14
criticism, committed inside R7-4's own remedy. **→ `\d{4}` is FORBIDDEN by name
in the pin's comment. No year carve-out at all is needed** (below); if one ever
becomes necessary it is a **literal list of years**, never a digit count.

### 5b · → THE SCOPE: *values, never names* — and it answers B10 in the same move

**B10 confirmed exactly.** `templateOf` (`verify-cutover.ts:388-395`) returns
**lines 56–164** of `book.astro`; the meta description is at **`:36-37`** and the
JSON-LD that re-uses it at **`:44-54`** (`description,` shorthand at `:49`).
Executed: `templateOf(book.astro).includes('About 30 minutes — the fee is
credited')` → **false**. **The copy that forced the re-scope lands in its own
replacement's blind spot** — R7-B's phrasing, and it is right.

**Neither `templateOf` nor the whole file. The corpus is `WEB_PROSE`'s
`TEMPLATE_PROSE` half, built as *"values, never names"*:**

```
TEMPLATE_PROSE(file) =
    the CONTENTS of string and template literals in frontmatter / <script>   // reaches :37 and :49
  ∪ visible TEXT of the template (tags, and with them every attribute, blanked)
  ∪ the values of HUMAN-READABLE attributes only: alt | title | aria-label | content
  , then the support phone blanked.
```
⚠️ **`placeholder` is deliberately NOT human-readable here** — that is what
`"123 Maple St"` is, and whitelisting it re-imports the noise.
⚠️ **Blank with spaces, preserving newlines.** `stripForUse` *deletes* import
lines, so a `file:line` computed from its output is **shifted from the real
source** — measured: `BookingForm.svelte:882` reports as `:625`. **Every offender
message must name a true source line.** *(A defect in the error message is how a
real finding gets dismissed as a stale citation.)*

**Executed on the five files: 0 matches — GREEN on arrival, with the support
phone as the ONLY carve-out.** Red-first, all planted in scratch copies and
asserted to have landed:

| Planted | prose corpus | visible-text only |
| --- | --- | --- |
| *"is free — normally 399."* in a text node | **RED** | RED |
| *"a 150 dollar travel fee"* in a text node | **RED** | RED |
| *"normally 399"* in the **meta description** `:37` | **RED** | 🔴 green ← B10's hole |
| *"normally 399"* in the **JSON-LD** `:48` | **RED** | 🔴 green ← B10's hole |
| `alt="399 dollar assessment…"` | **RED** | green |
| `const PRICE_399_LABEL_UNUSED = 1;` | ✅ **green** | green |
| `// normally 399 dollars` | ✅ **green** | green |

**That is the whole of B9's remedy: the shape `\b\d{3,}\b` was never the
problem.** ⚠️ **Assert the corpus is non-degenerate before believing the green** —
each file's corpus is 271–2534 non-space characters and contains known copy
(*"About 30 minutes"*, *"Book an Assessment"*); and assert it does **not**
contain `text-red-500`, `1024` or `123 Maple St`. **A corpus filter tight enough
to be green is one edit away from being empty.**

### 5c · 🔴 SF3 — the `.js` arm must be KEPT, and R7-B's numbers invert its own conclusion

| R7-B | Measured |
| --- | --- |
| *"5 of 6 client chunks"* match | 🔴 **6 of 25.** Five are minified artifacts (`$` as an identifier, `` `-$1` `` backreferences) |
| *"the day Vite co-locates `booking-copy.ts` with an island the ban goes red and the obvious fix deletes the arm"* | 🔴 **The five artifact chunks are NOT IN THE BAN'S SCOPE AND NEVER WERE.** `termsBundles` is content-selected and is **exactly one chunk today**, `booking-handoff.JaYwKbSR.js` — **which carries `$399`, `$699` and `$1,199` as real prices.** The `.js` arm is the only assertion that sees them |

**→ KEEP the arm. Tighten the SHAPE so a minified artifact cannot match it:**

```ts
// Real amounts only. `$1`–`$9` replace-backreferences and `$` as a minified
// identifier cannot match this; `\$\s?0\b` is explicit because a bare \d{2,}
// rule would quietly drop BK-48's $0 claim.
const DIST_AMOUNT = /\$\s?\d{2,}|\$\s?\d{1,3},\d{3}|\$\s?\d+\.\d{2}|\$\s?0\b/;
```
**Measured over all 42 `bundles`** — *`verify-cutover.ts:975-977`'s set: every `.js`/`.html` under `dist/client`, NOT every file, which `dist/client` has 49 text-bearing and 113 in total (R8-10f nit 9)* — **`/\$\s?[\d,]/` matches 9 with 5 false positives; `DIST_AMOUNT` matches 4 with ZERO.** The four are
`booking-handoff.*.js`, `book/index.html`, `index.html` and
`insurance-claims/index.html` — **all real.**

⚠️ 🔴 **`TERMS_MARKER` IS `FEE_TERMS_HEADING`'s VALUE — the literal string
`'Assessment terms'`** (`verify-cutover.ts:43`, `:1126`). **`termsBundles`, and
therefore `bookingSurfaces`'s whole `.js` half, is found by grepping the build
for that heading.** R6-2 keeps `FEE_TERMS_HEADING` unchanged, so the marker
survives — **but say so, because it is load-bearing and it is one wording change
away from `termsBundles.length === 0` and an inert arm.** ✅ The guard at
`:1136-1139` already fires in that case; **its existence is the reason this is a
statement and not a blocker.**

### 5d · `insurance-claims.astro` — a DECISION, not a structural accident

R6-3d and R7-B both record `insurance-claims` as *"excluded structurally"* and
*"unrelated, CORRECT copy"*. **Verified live and the framing needs correcting:**
`insurance-claims.astro:65` carries *"commonly **$500 to $2,500** in Alberta"* —
**in FRONTMATTER** (fences at 1 and 93), so `templateOf` cannot see it either,
and it renders to `dist/client/insurance-claims/index.html`.

**→ It stays OUT of scope, and the reason is now recorded rather than
inherited:** it is a range describing **what an insurance claim commonly costs**,
not what **we** charge. Decision 25 bans *our* prices from *our* funnel; this is
neither. **A structural exclusion that happens to be right is one refactor away
from being wrong — so the exclusion is by NAME, in the scope comment, with this
sentence as its reason.** ⚠️ **This is the one file where widening the dist scope
to all of `dist/client` would go red on arrival, which is why the scope is
enumerated (R8-1) rather than widened.**

## R8-6 · 🔴 B6, B7, B8 — THREE ASSERTION SITES OWNED BY NEITHER TICKET

**All three executed in a sandbox. Two of R7-B's three ownership claims are
right; one of its mechanisms is wrong in a way that changes the gate.**

### 6a · B6 — `verify-booking-email.ts:565-602`, the render-order chain

**Measured, instrumented:** the region runs **86 assertions**, not the *"2×2"*
R7-B implies — `:565-570` `carries` **54**, `:571-576` `mustNotCarry` **14**,
`:597-602` the chain **18** (5 links on `request`, 4 on `confirmed`; `INTRO` is
spliced in only for `request` at `:589-591`).

The chain reads six constants; **four are deleted** — `FEE_TERMS_INTRO` `:590`,
`FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT[0]` `:593`, `FEE_TERMS_REFUND[0]` `:594`, `FEE_TERMS_CREDIT`
`:595`.

🔴 **Two DIFFERENT failure classes, at two different commits, and they must not
be confused:**

| Commit | What happens | Is it a measurement? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **BK-55's (b)** — the render is deleted, the constants still exist | **exit 1, `✗ 20 checks failed`** — 12 from `carries`, **8 from the chain** (`PAYMENT` is `-1`, failing the `>` at its link and the `>= 0` guard at the next) | ✅ **YES.** Legible, and every other assertion still ran |
| **BK-53's** — the constants are deleted | `astro check`: **TS2459/TS2724 at `:30,:33,:35,:37,:38`.** Under `tsx`: `SyntaxError: … does not provide an export named 'FEE_TERMS_CREDIT'` **before one assertion runs — 0 `✗`, no summary line, all 417 `check()` calls skipped** | 🔴 **NO.** CLAUDE.md's BK-33 trap exactly |

**→ OWNER: BK-53, Class 1** — the chain asserts the constants' order *relative to
each other*, which is a composition claim (seam clause 5, the same rule that gave
BK-53 `:525-546`). It re-derives against `ASSESSMENT_TERMS`, e.g.
`HEADING → ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0] → FEE_TERMS_ITEMS[0] → ASSESSMENT_TERMS[6]`.
🔴 **BUT THE 8 REDS ARRIVE AT BK-55's COMMIT, AND THAT IS THE SEAM DEFECT — R8-6d.**

### 6b · B7 — `verify-booking-admin-db.ts:134` / `:2754`. **R7-B's mechanism is WRONG, and the correction is good news**

R7-B: *"it is a dynamic `await import()`, so the destructure yields `undefined`
and `escapeHtml(undefined)` throws **or** the negative at `:2754` passes
vacuously."*

| Claim | Verdict |
| --- | --- |
| dynamic `await import()` at `:134-136` | ✅ **confirmed** (bk51-gated `:134`; `main` `:134`) |
| `:2754` is a negative assertion | ✅ **confirmed** (`main` `:2675` — offset **+79**) |
| *"therefore no compile error"* | 🔴 **WRONG.** Executed: `astro check` reports **`ts(2339)` at `:134`** — `scripts/` is inside tsconfig's `**/*` include. **`npm run typecheck` goes red the moment BK-53 lands**, naming the file and the line |
| *"…**or** the negative passes vacuously"* | ⚠️ **Only the first branch is reachable.** `escapeHtml` is evaluated **before** `includes`, and executed, `escapeHtml(undefined)` **throws** `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')` (`booking-email.ts:282`). The vacuous pass never happens **here** |
| *"R6-0 says it was not run at baseline, so there is no green to compare against"* | ✅ **confirmed** — R6-0's table covers only typecheck, pricing and email |
| *(new)* the script's own instrumentation | ✅ **It already has `checksRun`, THREE terminal states and a `try`/`catch`** — exit 0 / 1 / **2 = crashed**, printing `✗ CRASHED after N checks`. **It is the best-instrumented of the three and R7-9's counter obligation does NOT apply to it** |

⚠️ **The shape difference matters for the audit method:** a dynamic import
produces **`TS2339` (property does not exist)**, not `TS2724` (no exported
member). **A traceability grep for *"no exported member"* misses this site.**

**→ OWNER: BK-53, Class 1.** Drop `FEE_TERMS_INTRO` from the `:134` destructure
and 🔴 **RE-AIM `:2754`, never delete it.** Its message — *"and NOT the sentence
promising a payment link to a customer who has just paid"* — is about **the
claim**, and the claim survives: `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[5]` is the successor
pre-payment sentence. **Deleting the row drops the only assertion anywhere that a
paid confirmation does not promise a payment link.** *B8's silent-narrowing
defect, in a file B8 does not mention.*

### 6c · B8 — `verify-booking-ics.ts`, and the ban that shrinks with its subjects

**Arithmetic confirmed exactly.** `BANNED` (`:748-763`) is **9 rows**; four are
deleted, leaving **5**. Assessment-terms subjects **6 → 2**. The loop is
5 messages × 3 parts × |BANNED|, so **135 → 75 assertions; assessment-terms
coverage 90 → 30 — and nothing goes red.**
✅ **R7-B's citation is precise:** `:752` **is** owned (R6-2's `FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT`
row names `verify-booking-ics.ts:45`/`:752`) and `:751` at R6-3e. **`:750`, `:753`,
`:754` are exactly and only the unowned three.**
✅ **The script's baseline is GREEN** — executed: exit 0, `✓ calendar invite
checks passed`, **502 `check()` calls.** *(R6-1 measured it exiting 1 with zero
`✗` under a BREAK, not at baseline. Both are true and they were being conflated.)*
🔴 **And the crash path is reproduced TWICE** — missing export → `SyntaxError`;
`FEE_TERMS_ITEMS = []` → `escapeHtml` throws at `:791`. **Both: exit 1, ZERO `✗`,
no summary line.**

**→ OWNER: BK-53** — every row reads a constant it deletes, and the boundary
builders (`planCancellationEmail`, `planRefundNotice`, `planRestoreEmail`,
`planFirstConfirmationEmail`) are ones BK-55 explicitly does not touch.
**→ THE FIX IS SHAPES, NOT VALUES**, because the banned copy survives as prose
inside `ASSESSMENT_TERMS` after the constants holding it are gone:

```ts
const BANNED: [string, string][] = [
  ['the terms heading', FEE_TERMS_HEADING], ['a terms item', FEE_TERMS_ITEMS[0]],
  ['the have-ready heading', HAVE_READY_HEADING], ['a have-ready item', HAVE_READY_ITEMS[0]],
  ['the GST registrant', GST_REGISTRATION_NUMBER],
];
// THE CLAIMS, not the constants carrying them: a value-keyed ban shrank from six
// subjects to two with nothing red. These survive the next rewording too.
const BANNED_SHAPES: [string, RegExp][] = [
  ['a payment-link promise',     /link you choose to pay|we email you a (?:secure )?(?:payment )?link/i],
  ['a slot-release warning',     /(?:we )?may release the (?:time|slot)/i],
  ['a refund term',              /refund(?:ed)? (?:you )?in full|is not refunded|full refund/i],
  ['the credit-to-invoice term', /comes off your final invoice|credited (?:in full )?against/i],
  ['a free-visit claim',         /the (?:on-site )?assessment (?:visit )?is free/i],
  ['a travel-fee disclosure',    /travel fee may apply/i],
  ['a no-obligation claim',      /no obligation to go ahead/i],
];
check(BANNED.length + BANNED_SHAPES.length === 12,
  'the boundary-message ban still covers twelve subjects — a row deleted with its constant is the BK-53 defect');
```
**Coverage becomes 12 × 15 = 180**, up from 135, instead of falling to 75.
⚠️ **The arity pin is the point.** *A deleted row is invisible; a deleted row
that changes a number is not.* **This is R8-4b's closure idea at assertion-list
scale, and the two should be read together.**
🔴 **`verify-booking-ics.ts` still gets R7-9's three-line counter AND a
`try`/`catch`/exit-2 wrapper copied from `verify-booking-admin-db.ts:237-259`,
`:5047-5068`.** Without both, **every red-first row scored against
`verify:booking:ics` is unsafe to believe** — including R6-4 row 6, which is
scored on this script today.

### 6d · 🔴 THE SEAM RULE PRODUCES A RED COMMIT, AND CLAUSE 5 IS WHY

**B5, sandboxed and confirmed: BK-55's commit (b) exits 1 with `✗ 20 checks
failed` — and all 20 are in arms clause 5 assigns to BK-53.** BK-55 §H-2
diagnosed this and blamed the fixture; **reversing A7 does not touch these 20**,
because they come from the render deletion.

**The two rules disagree:**

> **Clause 5** assigns an arm by **what it READS**. → BK-53.
> **The gate** requires every commit to pass. The arm is reddened by **what
> BK-55's commit DELETES**. → BK-55.

**When those differ, clause 5 produces a red commit — and a red commit cannot go
to implementation review, so the ticket cannot ship as specced.**

**→ 🆕 SEAM CLAUSE 6, and it is subordinating, not a carve-out:**

> **Clause 5 assigns the arm's DESIGN. It does not license a red commit. Where a
> ticket's own change reddens an arm clause 5 gives the other ticket, the
> reddening ticket makes the MINIMUM edit that restores green — deleting the rows
> whose subject its own commit removed, and nothing else — and the owning ticket
> re-derives the arm in its commit. An arm edited twice across two commits is
> correct; a red commit is not.**

**Applied, and it is small:** BK-55's commit (b) removes from `:525-546`'s
`carries` the four `FEE_TERMS_PAYMENT` members its render deletion drops, and
removes `['PAYMENT', …]` and `['REFUND', …]`… — 🔴 **no. `FEE_TERMS_REFUND` is
still RENDERED at BK-55's commit** (§B-2 deletes only the payment render), so
only `PAYMENT` leaves the chain, and `REFUND`'s link re-anchors to `ITEMS`.
**BK-55 §B-2 must state this arm edit explicitly and its red count is 20, not 0.**
⚠️ **N10 is right that BK-55's anticipated red count was understated — by 8 of
20, which is 40%.**

### R8-8a · 🔴 SF5 — A SINGLE PRE-REGISTERED COUNT CANNOT CATCH A NET-ZERO SWAP, AND A RANGE MAKES IT WORSE

R7-7 orders a **range** with stated bounds (correctly — the count is
build-dependent). **R7-7 row 19 then asks that range to catch "delete one arm and
add a `check(true)"* — a change whose net delta is ZERO.** A scalar cannot see it;
**a range sees it even less.** SF5 is right and the two paragraphs contradict each
other three apart.

**→ The pre-registration is PER NAMED PIN, not per script.** ⚠️ 🔴 **REWRITTEN revision 11 (R11-1d): this said "PER NAMED BLOCK", and "block" now means A-R9-2's extracted unit and nothing else.** The table below lists **pins**, not extracted regions — the collision became load-bearing when the coupling unit moved to the block.

| Registered unit | Why |
| --- | --- |
| coupling pin (source, per tier) · `dist/` arm · `MONEY_PROMISE` · W53-30 · `WEB_AMOUNT_BAN` · `WEB_CONSTANTS` closure · each re-pointed legacy block | Deleting an arm from one block changes **that block's** number even when the script total is unchanged |
| the **script total**, as a RANGE with each bound's stated cause | Still registered — it is what catches a whole block vanishing, and it is the number the summary line prints |

**Row 12 is scored on the PER-NAMED-PIN count. Row 19 is retired**, because with
per-pin counts it is row 12. *(⛔ ~~BLOCK count~~ / ~~per-block~~ — corrected R12-9 O5: "block" means A-R9-2's extracted unit and nothing else.)*
⚠️ **`verify-cutover.ts` loops per dist chunk, so the script total moves with a
Vite chunking change or one new blog post. Re-register after any dependency bump,
and record the cause beside the number** — *a number corrected without an
investigation is the defect this whole mechanism exists to prevent.*

### R8-10a · 🔴 T2 — THE THIRTEENTH SHAPE, AND IT IS DECISION 27 MADE ENFORCEABLE

**Decision 27: NO stated cancellation-notice expectation.** SF1's sentence is
exactly one, wearing a travel qualifier. **→ `MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES` gains:**

```ts
  // SF1: a cancellation-conditional charge is a money policy whatever it says
  // about the amount, and decision 27 says the site states none. The travel
  // qualifier must not launder it -- so this is an OUTRIGHT ban, and a sentence
  // matching it is red even with the qualifier present.
  /\bcancel(?:l?ing|l?ation)?\b[^.<>]{0,60}?\b(?:charged?|charges|fee|refund|penalty|no cost)\b/i,
```
✅ **Verified it does NOT match `CANCEL_LINE`** — *"To cancel or reschedule, call
or text …"* carries no money noun within 60 characters.
🔴 **And it is an OUTRIGHT ban, like every `MONEY_PROMISE` shape (A-R7-2): a
sentence matching it is red even carrying the travel qualifier.** *That is the
whole of SF1 — the qualifier made the weaker rule apply. A ban with no qualified
form cannot be laundered by one.*
**→ Red-first row 28.**

# 🟥 REVISION 7 — 2026-09-06. ⛔ **SUPERSEDED BY REVISION 8. NOT THE SPEC.**

> 🔴 **Read revision 8 first.** Three of this revision's designs were measured
> broken: `MONEY_PROMISE`/W30's scope (**R8-1**), the `dist/` per-sentence rule
> (**R8-2**), and the coupling rule's own unit and amend-list (**R8-3**). Its
> **red-first table is struck — R8-8 is the only one.** Everything not
> contradicted by R8-0's absorption table still stands and is still cited by it.

Written against the traceability audit (**R7-A**) and adversarial pre-read
(**R7-B**) of revision 6. **5 adversarial blockers + 8 audit blockers**, nine of
them **executed** rather than argued.

🔴 **The audit's diagnosis, adopted as a standing rule:**

> **When a section is replaced wholesale, enumerate the OLD section's rows
> against the new one and state which verdicts changed.**

R6 did that for R5-12's *findings* and not for R5-4/R5-5's own *content* — so six
rows fell through a table that never claimed to cover them. **R7-0 is that
enumeration**, and it is the deliverable that stops this recurring. *(It is
CLAUDE.md's rule-rewrite trap, one level up: applied to sections, not rules.)*

## R7-1 · 🔴 THE RADIO PRICES — restored to the copy AND the gate

**Dropped by revision 6 after being a blocker in three rounds (R1-A1 → R4-2 →
R5-5c). It is the most visible price surface on the site.**

**Verified:** `BookingForm.svelte:1173` renders
`{formatCents(tierQuotes[tier].baseCents)} + GST` inside its own `{#if tierQuotes}`
(`:1171-1175`) within `{#each ASSESSMENT_TIERS}` (`:1154-1182`) — **not** inside
the `{#if quote}` block at `:1198-1217`. And `verify-cutover.ts:597`
**positively requires** the string `island.includes('formatCents(tierQuotes[tier].baseCents)')`,
as the third clause of a triple-AND at `:594-598`.

**→ COPY: delete `BookingForm.svelte:1171-1175`.** The tier LABEL stays.
**→ GATE: re-point `:594-598`** to assert `formatCents(tierQuotes` is **ABSENT**
from the island while `ASSESSMENT_TIER_NAMES[tier]` is still present — a negative
**with** its positive twin.
**→ KEEP `:600-603`** (*"the island contains no price arithmetic of its own"*) —
**more** valuable once no price renders (R3B-D3).
⚠️ **`tierQuotes` then has no reader in the template.** `noUnusedLocals` is not
set, so pin its absence explicitly.

## R7-2 · 🔴 THE GATE HAS NO REFUND OR CREDIT BAN — and the ticket's title is *"stop promising a refund"*

**Executed.** All four money-promise claims match **zero** of the ten
`FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES` and zero of the four `WEB_AMOUNT_SHAPES`:

| Restored claim | coupling | amount |
| --- | --- | --- |
| *"Cancel 24 hours or more before your appointment and we refund you in full."* | GREEN | GREEN |
| *"Inside 24 hours, the assessment is not refunded."* | GREEN | GREEN |
| *"the full amount comes off your final invoice"* | GREEN | GREEN |
| `llms.txt`'s *"The assessment fee is credited in full against the final invoice"* | GREEN | GREEN |

**And `verify-cutover.ts` contains no refund pin to inherit** — grepped: every
occurrence of `refund`/`credit` is a **comment** or the ordering message at
`:369`, and R6-3e deletes or re-points all three constant names that carry them.
**After BK-53 the file has nothing that can see a refund promise.**

🔴 **This is CLAUDE.md's negative-assertion trap in its exact BK-48 shape** — the
pins are *named* for free claims and every reviewer reads them as covering the
money-promise category. **And the adversary is not hypothetical: all four
sentences are the deleted `FEE_TERMS_REFUND`/`FEE_TERMS_CREDIT` text verbatim, so
the break is one `git revert` of a constant.**

**→ W29 · `MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES`, banned outright on every web surface** (there
is no qualifier that makes them true — the assessment is free, so there is
nothing to refund or credit):

```ts
const MONEY_PROMISE_SHAPES = [
  /\brefunds?(?:ed|able)?\b/i,
  /\bcredit(?:ed|s)?\b[^.<>]{0,40}?\b(?:invoice|final bill|against|back)\b/i,
  /\bcomes? off\b[^.<>]{0,30}?\b(?:invoice|bill|total)\b/i,
  /\bmoney[\s-]?back\b/i,
  /\bdeposit\b/i,
];
```
⚠️ **Scope: the web surfaces of R7-3c, NOT `booking-copy.ts` wholesale** — the
twelve email lifecycle constants (`REFUND_TIMING_LINE`, `REFUNDED_LEAD`,
`refundedAmountLine()`, …) legitimately say *refund* about **real refunds of real
charges** and are KEEP-UNCHANGED (BK-55 §B-5). **Per-constant, per
`verify-booking-email.ts:368-372`'s existing pattern (R3B-C2).**
**→ Red-first row 15: restore `FEE_TERMS_REFUND[1]` → `MONEY_PROMISE` red.**

## R7-3 · 🔴 THE COUPLING PIN — per SENTENCE, because decision 17a says so

### 3a · Red-first rows 1 and 2 are GREEN. **Executed, twice, independently.**

Deleting `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[1]` and changing nothing else leaves the pin **green**,
because `FEE_TERMS_ACK_LABEL` still says *"a travel fee may apply"* and R6-3a
joins it into `TERMS_TEXT`. **The row that validates the whole coupling design is
the one break it cannot see.**

🔴 **And a blob is the wrong unit on the merits, not just for the break row.**
**Decision 17a requires the qualifier IN THE SAME SENTENCE as the free claim.**
Executed: moving the travel clause out of `ASSESSMENT_TERMS` and into
`ASSESSMENT_TIER_DESCRIPTIONS.sketch` also stays **green** — the customer reads
*"The on-site assessment visit is free."* at the top of the box while the
qualifier sits inside one of three radio descriptions.

**→ The unit is the SENTENCE.** Split each constant on `/(?<=[.!?])\s+/`, and for
every sentence matching a `FREE_CLAIM_SHAPE`, require `TRAVEL_QUALIFIER` **in
that sentence or the one immediately following it within the same constant.**
✅ Assert at least one sentence matches a free shape — **a positive twin**, so the
pin cannot pass by matching nothing (R7-B nit 5: *"assert both sides non-empty"*
was ambiguous and vacuous on its natural reading).

### 3b · Two drafted sentences must change — decision 17a requires it

Under a per-sentence rule these are non-compliant **as approved**, which is a
decision-17a correction rather than a taste change:

- **`ASSESSMENT_TIER_NOTE`** → *"The visit is free whichever you pick — a travel
  fee may apply further out. Picking one just tells us what to prepare; anything
  written comes later, with your quote."*
- **`FEE_TERMS_ITEMS[0]`** → drop the bare *"**Free.**"*. `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0]`
  already states it **with** its qualifier two lines above, and a bare *"Free."*
  cannot carry one without absurdity. ⚠️ **This also retires
  `FREE_CLAIM_SHAPES[2]`, which R7-B SF-8 measured DEAD inside the join anyway**
  (`join(' ')` puts a letter after the space so neither `$` nor the closing-quote
  class can match). **Keep the shape for the `dist/` arm, where `</li>` supplies
  the `<`; drop it from the source arm and say why.**
- ⚠️ **Join with `'\n'`, not `' '`** — R7-B nit 4 measured the blob manufacturing
  a claim across a boundary (`"Assessment terms The on-site assessment visit is
  free"` spanning `FEE_TERMS_HEADING` + `ASSESSMENT_TERMS[0]`). Sentence-splitting
  makes this moot; the newline makes it impossible.

### 3c · 🔴 The scan misses the surfaces the ticket actually edits

R6-3a covers six `booking-copy.ts` constants. **R6-2 puts drafted copy on three
surfaces that are not constants at all:** `book.astro:36-37`/`:94`,
`ContactSection.astro:287`, `llms.txt.ts:54`, `[service].astro:79`.

**→ `WEB_CLAIM_SURFACES` — the source arm scans, per rendered template:**
`book.astro`, `src/sections/ContactSection.astro`, `BookingForm.svelte`,
`llms.txt.ts`, `[service].astro` — through `stripForUse` + `normalise` +
`templateOf` where applicable.
⚠️ **R7-B SF-2:** red-first row 8 (*"drop the travel clause from
`[service].astro:79`"*) was attributed to the coupling pin, **which structurally
cannot see a template**. With this arm it can. **Verified: all four built service
pages carry `no obligation` today with no qualifier — they are RED ON ARRIVAL
until `[service].astro:79` lands.** That is the correct order and must be stated.

### 3d · Both twins, not one

R6-3b specified only the **source** twin while red-first row 3 predicted the
**`dist/`** twin's behaviour (*"the dist twin alone stays green"*).
**→ Specify both:** `dist/client/index.html` and `dist/client/book/index.html`
each carry a free claim **and** a qualifier (R5-5a A5, restored), **and** the
source twin of R6-3b. **Neither alone is sufficient** — R7-B measured that the
`dist/` twin passes when the eyebrow is deleted, because the terms box on the same
built page carries the claim.

## R7-4 · 🔴 `WEB_AMOUNT_BAN` — scope and shapes, both too narrow

**Enumerated against the real `dist/`:** `bookingSurfaces`
(`verify-cutover.ts:1128-1135`) is `dist/client/book/**.html` +
`dist/client/index.html` + the terms `.js` chunks. **Out of scope and edited by
this ticket:** `dist/client/llms.txt` *(never in `bundles` — it is neither `.html`
nor `.js`)*, the **four** `[service]` pages, and `contact/index.html`
*(`ContactSection.astro:287` renders on `/contact/` too — `contact.astro:82`)*.

🔴 **`llms.txt` is the sharpest of the three**, and `verify-cutover.ts:1188-1191`'s
own comment says why: it is *"the file an AI assistant quotes with no page around
it to qualify the claim."*

**→ Scope = `bookingSurfaces` ∪ `dist/client/llms.txt` ∪ the four
`dist/client/<service>/index.html` ∪ `dist/client/contact/index.html`.**
**Enumerate what the scope EXCLUDES, in the pin's comment** — `insurance-claims`
(correct `$500`–`$2,500`), `about`, `blog/**`, `404` — so the next reader sees the
boundary rather than re-deriving it.

**And the shapes miss ordinary spellings (R7-B SF-4, executed):**
`"a 150 dollar travel fee"` (singular), `"a travel fee of CAD 150"` (number
second), `"Normally 399, free now"` (no `$`, no `+ GST`) all pass.
**→** add `/\b\d[\d,]*\s*dollars?\b/i`, `/\bCAD\s*\d/i`, and — inside
`WEB_CLAIM_SURFACES` only — a bare `/\b\d{3,}\b/` with the four-digit year and the
phone number excluded by name.
⚠️ *"Shaped for the three prices being removed rather than for the category"* is
the A14 criticism R6-3e levels at `!figures.includes('399')`, **reproduced one
section later in `WEB_AMOUNT_SHAPES` itself.**

loops per dist chunk, so a Vite chunking change or one new blog post moves it, and
it gets *"corrected"* rather than investigated. **→ Pre-register a RANGE with a
stated cause for each bound, and re-register after any dependency bump.**
